CHAPTER 5Robotics Explained

Introduction

Now that we have explored blockchain and AI, let us understand robotics.

In this chapter, we are going to explore where mechanical engineering, electronic engineering, and computer science converge, transforming our lives in more ways than we often realize—robotics. Robots are no longer just the stuff of science fiction. They are here, and they are becoming increasingly integral to our everyday lives. From manufacturing our goods and taking care of our health to helping us around the house and even exploring the farthest reaches of space, robots are stepping in to do the tasks that are dangerous, dull, or simply beyond human capabilities. They are enhancing efficiency, precision, and safety across various sectors, making our lives easier and safer.

The evolution from the first visions of automated machines to the complex robots we see today is driven by continuous advancements in technology, engineering, and computer science. That's a fascinating journey marked by incredible innovations, ambitious dreams, and the tireless efforts of scientists and engineers around the globe. This chapter will explore robotics’ history, significance, design and functionality in detail. We will also understand various use cases and look at some of the popular robots worldwide. Lastly, we will also discuss about a few potential career opportunities for those who want to enter this transformative field.

Structure

In this chapter, we will cover the following topics:

  • Introducing robotics
  • Exploring the significance of robotics
  • Robotics and its history
  • Iterations in robotics design and functionality
  • Industrial use cases of robotics
  • Domestic use cases of robotics
  • Famous robots around the world
  • Career in robotics

Objectives

In this chapter, we are going to explore the fascinating evolution of robotics, from its earliest origins to the advanced, learning machines we see today. We will also understand why we need robots, how they have transformed from simple automated machines to complex entities, and the key milestones that have shaped this field.

However, our journey does not stop at history. We are also going to explore how robotics technology has revolutionized various industries and our daily lives. From manufacturing and logistics to healthcare and agriculture, robots are making a significant impact. They are not just confined to factories and fields—they are also helping us in our homes, assisting with tasks, and even providing entertainment in the form of robotic toys.

By the end of this chapter, we hope you will have a comprehensive understanding of the field of robotics, and you will appreciate the potential of robotics technology and its impact on various sectors and everyday life.

Introducing robotics

Today, robotics is one of the most intriguing and rapidly evolving fields in technology. It converges technologies like computer engineering, artificial intelligence, and automation to create highly advanced automated machines that can perform sophisticated tasks in a controlled manner. Robots have infiltrated many areas of our lives and are here to evolve, whether we realize it or not.

A robot typically consists of several key components that allow it to interact and respond to its environment:

  • Sensors: Robots use advanced sensors like cameras, Light Detection and Ranging (LiDAR), infrared and ultrasonic sensors, pressure detectors, etc., to sense and interact with their surroundings.
  • Actuators: These function as robotic muscles and convert electrical inputs into actual movements. Mechanical parts such as motors, servos, and pneumatic actuators help in robotic arm movements.
  • Control systems: They are the brain of the robots that process data and provide relevant output. The software enables real-time decision making, allowing robots to adapt and perform tasks with precision.
  • Power supply: Robots require extensive power for mobility and autonomy. Fuel cells often provide longer-lasting energy. Though power cables provide reliable electricity, they limit movement.
  • Communication systems: These allow robots to interact with humans or other robots effectively via wireless technologies like Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, or custom protocols. Such systems allow real-time decision making and coordination during complex tasks.

  • Safety mechanisms: Advanced, automated technology like robotics is often followed by immense safety concerns. Emergency stop buttons, fail-safes, and safety barriers are crucial to shut down a robot in case of emergencies, system failures, or mishaps.

    Source: https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/building-blocks-robotics-look-key-components-nextup-community-y6alf/

All these components work together to create highly perceptive robots that can process information, think, and respond accordingly. Let us move further and explore the importance of robotics.

Exploring the significance of robotics

Robotics is important in today’s world because the times we are heading towards with automation facilitated by technologies like blockchain and AI, require it to give the future its proper shape.

Once we explore the evolution of robotics fully, from simple machines to complex, learning entities, we will see how robots have become more efficient, versatile, and intelligent over time, and how they are changing the world around us. Robotics is important not only because of the technical side of things, though. Along with making a real difference in big industries like manufacturing, logistics, healthcare, and agriculture, robots are also making their way into our homes, helping us with our daily chores, and even keeping us company.

Importance of robotics

Let us talk about the incredible world of robotics and why it is becoming such a big deal in so many sectors.

Robots are not just those cool sci-fi characters we see in movies. They can work non-stop without getting tired, handle tasks that might be dangerous for us, and they are super precise. Here is why they are important:

  • Take manufacturing, for instance. Back in the day, humans did everything, but now, robots are the stars of the show. They are assembling, painting, welding, and even packaging. The best part is that they are super-fast, make fewer mistakes, and never call in sick. Plus, there is this new breed of robots called cobots – they are like the friendly neighbors of the robot world. They work right next to us, lending a hand (or a mechanical arm) when needed.
  • Now, let us talk about healthcare. Robots are making waves here too. The Da Vinci Surgical System pictured in the following Figure 5.1 is a super advanced robot that assists surgeons in performing intricate procedures. It is so precise that patients often recover faster and with fewer complications. Robots are also helping patients get back on their feet after accidents or illnesses. They are like high-tech physiotherapists.

Figure 5.1: Three components of the da Vinci system
Source: https://www.intuitive.com/en-us/patients/da-vinci-robotic-surgery/about-the-systems

  • Agriculture's getting a tech makeover too. Robots are out in the fields planting, harvesting, and even keeping an eye on crops. They are like the modern-day scarecrows, but way smarter. With all their sensors and AI, they give farmers real-time updates, helping them make the best decisions for their crops. It is all about growing more while taking care of Mother Earth.
  • Let us talk about logistics. Robots are in warehouses picking, packing, and sorting. Day or night, they are making sure our packages are ready to go. With drones and autonomous delivery bots on the horizon, our packages might just land on our doorsteps even faster.
  • Robots are also making their mark in construction, mining, defense, and even space exploration. Whether it is laying bricks, exploring Mars, or defusing bombs, robots are there, making things safer and more efficient for all of us.

So, in a nutshell, robots are not just machines; they are our partners in progress. They are helping us do more, reach further, and dream bigger.

Robotics and its history

Let us explore the history of robotics and explore when we first saw something closely resembling today’s robots and how robotics evolved over many years.

Early beginnings of robotics

Creating machines that can perform tasks autonomously has been a source of human fascination for centuries. This fascination has its roots in the ancient world, where inventors and engineers crafted automata—mechanical devices that could perform simple tasks or mimic human actions. These early machines were the precursors to the robots we know today, and their creation marked the beginning of humanity's journey toward the development of modern robotics. Let us now talk about the early machines which could carry out various tasks:

  • In ancient Greece, the engineer Heron of Alexandria pioneered the creation of automata, as is referenced in the following Figure 5.2. He designed various devices, including a steam-powered contraption that could open temple doors and a mechanical play that could run for ten minutes. While simple by today's standards, these creations were revolutionary for their time. They demonstrated that machines could be designed to perform tasks without human intervention, laying the groundwork for developing autonomous machines.

Figure 5.2: Heron of Alexandria’s diagram of an automaton dispensing wine and milk in a temple
Source: https://www.historyofinformation.com/image.php?id=3851

  • Similarly, in ancient China, inventors crafted mechanical figurines that could serve tea or play music. Like those of Heron of Alexandria, these automata were powered by simple mechanisms such as weights, springs, and water. They could only perform pre-programmed tasks, and the concept of a machine that could think and learn like a human was still a distant dream. However, these early automata represented an essential step in the evolution of robotics, demonstrating the potential of machines to mimic human actions and perform tasks autonomously.
  • The journey towards modern robotics took a significant leap forward in the 18th and 19th centuries, with the development of programmable machines such as the Jacquard loom and the analytical engine. These machines used punched cards to control their operations, a concept that would later be integral to developing the first computers.

The Jacquard loom, for instance, could weave complex patterns based on the instructions encoded in the punched cards, demonstrating the potential of machines to perform complex tasks with precision and consistency. You can find it pictured in Figure 5.3:

Figure 5.3: Jacquard loom in the National Museum of Scotland
Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacquard_machine#/media/File:A_Jacquard_loom_showing_information_punchcards,_National_Museum_of_Scotland.jpg

  • As mentioned back in Chapter 3, AI Explained, in Karel Čapek's 1920 play Rossum's Universal Robots (R.U.R.), the term robot was introduced. The play portrayed a future where artificial humans, also known as robots, were employed as low-cost labor. This dystopian vision of a world dominated by robots sparked a rebellion leading to the extinction of the human race, highlighting the potential dangers of unchecked technological advancement.
  • The first actual industrial robot, the Unimate, was installed on a General Motors assembly line in 1961. The Unimate was a programmable machine that could perform repetitive tasks such as welding and moving parts. Its success marked a turning point in the history of robotics. For the first time, a robot was being used in a real-world industrial setting, performing tasks previously done by humans. This marked the beginning of the modern era of robotics, paving the way for the widespread adoption of robots in manufacturing and other industries.

You can find the Unimate picture in the following Figure 5.4:

Figure 5.4: Unimate
Source: https://artsandculture.google.com/asset/the-unimate-robot/6QHAyki7K0pjZA)

The early beginnings of robotics, from ancient automata to the first industrial robots, represent a journey of human ingenuity and innovation. They reflect our enduring fascination with creating machines that can perform tasks autonomously, a fascination that has driven the evolution of robotics from simple mechanical devices to today's sophisticated, intelligent robots.

As we continue to explore the potential of robotics, these early beginnings remind us how far we have come and hint at the exciting possibilities that lie ahead!

Robotics over the years

The shifts in robotics over the years since the ancient automata is a testament to human innovation and the relentless pursuit of technological advancement. From the introduction of the Unimate, the world's first industrial robot, to today's sophisticated, intelligent robots, the journey of robotics has been marked by significant milestones and transformative breakthroughs.

Here is a look at robotics over the years:

  • The 1970s and 1980s marked a pivotal period in the evolution of robotics. During this time, robots transitioned from rigid, inflexible machines to more versatile and intelligent devices. The Stanford Arm and the Programmable Universal Machine for Assembly (PUMA) were prime examples of this shift. These robots, capable of performing precise, complex tasks, demonstrated the potential of robotics in various applications.

    The following Figure 5.5 shows PUMA:

    Figure 5.5: PUMA
    Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Programmable_Universal_Machine_for_Assembly#/media/File:Unimate_500_PUMA_Deutsches_Museum.jpg

    Concurrently, the development of microprocessors and software revolutionized the control mechanisms of robots, enabling more flexible and intelligent operations.

  • As we moved into the 1990s and 2000s, robots began to venture out of the factory setting and into the wider world. This period saw the emergence of robots in everyday life and sensitive applications. The Roomba vacuum cleaner, for instance, revolutionized home cleaning. At the same time, the Da Vinci Surgical System brought unprecedented precision and control to surgical procedures. Meanwhile, the Mars rovers Spirit and Opportunity showcased the potential of robots in exploration and scientific research, opening new frontiers in space exploration. These Mars rovers can be seen in the following Figure 5.6:

Figure 5.6: Mars rover twins: Spirit and Opportunity
Source: https://science.nasa.gov/mission/mars-exploration-rovers-spirit-and-opportunity/)

  • The most recent years have witnessed yet another revolution in the field of robotics, driven by advancements in artificial intelligence. Today's robots can learn from experience, adapt to new situations, and even understand natural language. AI has opened new possibilities for robots, from self-driving cars and delivery drones to personal assistant robots and robotic companions. These advancements are transforming industries and redefining what robots can do.

Despite these significant advancements, the field of robotics is still in its early stages. There are numerous challenges to overcome, both technical and societal. Improving robot dexterity and autonomy remains a significant challenge on the technical front. Robots must be able to navigate complex, unpredictable environments and perform delicate tasks requiring high precision and control.

On the societal front, ensuring robots' safe and ethical use is a pressing issue. As robots become more integrated into our lives, we must consider the ethical implications and establish guidelines to ensure that the benefits of robotics are realized in a way that puts humans first.

The potential of robotics is vast, and the journey ahead will be exciting and transformative. As we continue to push the boundaries of what robots can do, we expect to see further advancements that will redefine our understanding of robotics and its role in our lives.

Iterations in robotics design and functionality

The new iterations we are seeing in robotics are the result of multifaceted evolution and here are the different kinds.

Flexibility revolution

The clunky, rigid robots of yesteryear have undergone a massive transformation. Today's robots are all about adaptability and versatility. They are designed to be the ultimate problem solvers, ready to tackle diverse challenges. There are two types of robots such as:

  • Modular robots: Think of these as the transformers in the robotics realm. They can reshape and reconfigure based on the task. Whether it is crawling, flying, or swimming, these robots adjust accordingly. It is not just about physical adaptability; it is about optimizing performance for varied tasks.
  • Soft robots: These are not your stereotypical metal behemoths. Crafted from flexible materials, they can squeeze, stretch, and adapt. These are perfect for navigating unpredictable environments or handling delicate items with utmost care.

Advanced sensory capabilities

Modern robots are incredibly perceptive. Equipped with advanced sensors, they can interpret their surroundings with astonishing accuracy, making them more responsive and intuitive. Vision and tactile sensors help robots to navigate, understand, and analyze their environment better. Let us understand them briefly:

  • Vision sensors: It is like giving robots the power of sight. They can now discern objects and interpret signs. This visual prowess enables them to navigate complex terrains and interact more naturally.
  • Tactile sensors: This is all about touch. Robots can now feel textures, gauge pressure, and handle objects with a precision that rivals human touch. It is revolutionizing industries, from manufacturing to healthcare.

Adaptable and self-improving

Robots have become avid learners. No longer confined to pre-set instructions, they are constantly evolving, adapting, and improving.

  • Artificial intelligence: With AI, robots can process vast amounts of data, learn from experiences, and make informed decisions. It is like equipping them with a dynamic brain that evolves with each task.
  • Adaptive behavior: Modern robots learn from their mistakes, ensuring they do not repeat them. This adaptability makes them invaluable in tasks that require constant optimization and improvement.

Expanding capabilities beyond physical tasks

Robots have transcended their traditional roles. They are not just about physical tasks anymore; they are thinkers, innovators, and problem solvers. Robots can make informed decisions and carry out complex tasks easily. Let us understand this briefly:

  • Decision making: Owing to machine learning, robots can sift through complex data, identify patterns, and make critical decisions. From healthcare diagnostics to financial forecasting, they are proving their mettle.
  • Complex tasks: Robots are now venturing into domains we once thought were exclusively human. Whether it is intricate surgical procedures, autonomous driving, or artistic endeavors, they are showcasing their versatility.

As we stand on the cusp of a new technological era, it is evident that robots will play an even more integral role in our lives. They are evolving from mere tools to partners, collaborators, and even companions. Robots are being designed to work in tandem with humans, understanding our needs, emotions, and aspirations. Combined with other emerging technologies like AI and blockchain, the realm of robotics is boundless. With each passing day, we are discovering new applications, pushing the boundaries of what is possible.

Industrial use cases of robotics

The industrial use of robotics has seen a significant surge in recent years, with robots being incorporated into various sectors such as manufacturing, logistics, healthcare, and agriculture. The application of robotics in these industries has increased efficiency, productivity, and safety, while also opening new possibilities for innovation and growth.

Manufacturing

In the manufacturing sector, robotics has been transforming the way products are assembled, inspected, and packaged. Industrial robots, designed to perform various tasks quickly, precisely, and consistently, are now integral to many manufacturing processes. They are used in tasks such as assembly, painting, welding, packaging, and inspection, performing them with speed and accuracy that surpasses human capabilities.

For instance, automobile manufacturers like Toyota and General Motors extensively use robots in their assembly lines. These robots perform tasks such as welding car bodies, installing windshields, and painting vehicles. They can work continuously without breaks, leading to increased productivity and efficiency. Moreover, using robots eliminates the risk of human error, ensuring high quality and consistency in the final product.

Another example is Foxconn, the world's largest contract electronics manufacturer, which uses robots to assemble products like iPhones and iPads. These robots can perform delicate tasks such as installing tiny screws with precision and consistency. Using robots in such precision-oriented tasks not only ensures the quality of the product but also significantly increases the speed of production.

Manufacturing robots increase productivity and improve safety by performing dangerous tasks that could put human workers at risk. They can also work in environments that are hazardous to humans, such as those with high temperatures or toxic substances. Robots are invaluable in industries where safety and efficiency are paramount.

Logistics

The logistics industry, a critical backbone of global commerce, is undergoing a significant transformation, thanks to the advent of robotics. Robots are playing an increasingly pivotal role in this sector, automating tasks such as sorting, packing, and transporting goods. This automation is not just about replacing manual labor; it is about enhancing efficiency, reducing delivery times, and ultimately, boosting customer satisfaction.

Robots in the logistics industry are tireless workers. They operate around the clock without breaks, fatigue, or loss of focus. This nonstop operation leads to a significant increase in efficiency, enabling logistics companies to handle larger volumes of goods and fulfill orders more quickly.

The result is a win-win situation for both the companies and their customers: companies can handle more business, and customers receive their orders faster.

Amazon, the world's largest online retailer, provides a prime example of how robots are revolutionizing logistics. In its vast warehouses, Amazon employs a fleet of robots developed by Amazon Robotics. These robots are not just simple machines; they are intelligent entities that use machine learning algorithms to optimize their routes and avoid obstacles. They move goods around the warehouse, picking up heavy loads and transporting them to their designated locations quickly and efficiently. This automation significantly reduces the time required to fulfill customer orders, leading to faster delivery times and increased customer satisfaction.

However, the role of robots in logistics is not confined to the warehouse. They are also making their mark in the delivery process. Companies like Starship Technologies and Nuro are developing small, self-driving robots that can deliver goods right to customers' doorsteps. These robots are designed to navigate sidewalks and crosswalks, making them ideal for last-mile deliveries in urban areas. They can operate independently, avoiding obstacles and safely reaching their destinations. This autonomous delivery capability increases efficiency, reduces the cost of delivery, and provides customers with a unique and convenient delivery experience.

The use of robots in the logistics industry is a testament to the transformative power of technology. It is not just about replacing human labor; it is about enhancing the entire logistics process, making it more efficient, reliable, and customer friendly. As these robots become more advanced and their use becomes more widespread, we can expect to see even more significant improvements in the logistics industry.

Healthcare

Robots are now playing a crucial role in various aspects of healthcare, from patient care to surgery, and even rehabilitation therapy. The integration of robotics in healthcare is not only improving the efficiency and effectiveness of care but also enhancing patient experiences and outcomes.

In patient care, robots are proving to be invaluable allies for healthcare professionals. They are taking over routine tasks, allowing healthcare professionals to focus more on direct patient care. A prime example of this is the Moxi robot developed by Diligent Robotics.

Moxi is designed to assist healthcare professionals by performing tasks such as delivering supplies, collecting laundry, and disposing of waste. By taking over these mundane tasks, Moxi frees up valuable time for healthcare professionals, allowing them to concentrate on providing high-quality patient care. This not only improves the efficiency of healthcare delivery, but also enhances the patient’s experience by ensuring that healthcare professionals can devote more attention to their patients.

You can see Moxi pictured in the following Figure 5.7:

Figure 5.7: Moxi robots
Source: https://www.diligentrobots.com/moxi

The impact of robotics is not limited to patient care; it extends to surgical procedures, rehabilitation, and administrative tasks. In the field of surgery, robots are revolutionizing how operations are performed. The Da Vinci Surgical System mentioned above, developed by Intuitive Surgical, is a case in point. This robotic system is designed to facilitate minimally invasive surgeries. It provides surgeons with enhanced vision, precision, and control, enabling them to perform complex surgical procedures with greater accuracy. The result is fewer complications, less postoperative pain for patients, and faster recovery times. This represents a significant advancement in surgical care, improving patient outcomes and enhancing the quality of care.

Robots are also making a significant impact in the field of rehabilitation therapy. The Lokomat, developed by Hocoma, is a robotic device designed to help patients with spinal cord injuries or strokes regain their walking abilities. The Lokomat provides repetitive, high-intensity training, a crucial component of neurorehabilitation. By providing consistent and intensive training, the Lokomat helps patients recover their motor skills more quickly and effectively. This not only improves patient outcomes but also enhances their quality of life. You can see the Lokomat in the following Figure 5.8:

Figure 5.8: Lokomat
Source: https://www.hocoma.com/solutions/lokomat/)

Agriculture

The agriculture industry, one of the oldest and most essential sectors of human civilization, is also undergoing a significant transformation. The integration of robotics into farming practices is revolutionizing the way we grow and harvest our food, bringing about a new era of efficiency, productivity, and sustainability.

Robots in agriculture are designed to automate a variety of tasks that were traditionally performed by human labor. These tasks include planting seeds, harvesting crops, and weeding fields. The beauty of these robots is their ability to work tirelessly, around the clock, without the need for breaks or rest. This continuous operation significantly increases productivity, allowing more work to be done in less time. It also reduces the need for manual labor, addressing the labor shortage issue that many agricultural regions face.

Take, for example, the Lettuce Bot developed by Blue River Technology. This innovative robot is designed to thin lettuce fields, a task that is crucial for ensuring optimal growth but is also time-consuming and labor-intensive. The Lettuce Bot uses machine vision technology to identify individual lettuce plants and then removes excess plants with precision. This process ensures that the remaining plants have enough space and resources to grow, leading to healthier crops and higher yields.

Another fascinating example is the Harvest CROO robot developed by Harvest CROO Robotics. This robot is designed to pick strawberries, a task that requires a high level of precision and delicacy. The Harvest CROO robot uses a combination of machine vision and machine learning algorithms to identify ripe strawberries and then carefully pick them without causing any damage to the fruit or the plant. This automation not only increases efficiency but also ensures the quality of the harvested strawberries.

Beyond increasing productivity, robots in agriculture are also contributing to the sustainability of farming practices. By performing tasks with a high level of precision, these robots can reduce the use of resources, such as water and fertilizers. They can target specific plants or areas, minimizing waste and reducing the environmental impact of farming. This precision farming approach is a significant step towards more sustainable and efficient agricultural practices.

Domestic use cases of robotics

The domestic sphere has seen a significant increase in the use of robotics right alongside the industrial counterpart, transforming everyday tasks and introducing a new level of convenience and efficiency. From robotic vacuum cleaners and personal assistant robots to robotic toys, the application of robotics in domestic settings is diverse and continually expanding.

Robotic vacuum cleaners

We all know how taxing and time-consuming cleaning our homes can be. This is where robotic vacuum cleaners come into the picture and helps you keep your floors clean without lifting a finger.

These handy little devices, like the popular Roomba series from iRobot (pictured in Figure 5.9), are a fantastic example of how robotics has made its way into our homes, making our lives a bit easier. Imagine this, while you are out running errands, working, or even sleeping, these robotic vacuum cleaners are hard at work. They are smart, using a combination of sensors, algorithms, and even machine learning to navigate around your home, avoid obstacles, and clean your floors.

Figure 5.9: Roomba
Source: https://www.inc.com/graham-winfrey/3-design-tips-from-the-revolutionary-roomba-vacuum.html)

Here is how they work: Roombas move around in a random or algorithmic pattern to ensure they cover the entire floor space. They are quite smart too—they can detect dirty spots on the floor and focus on areas that require more cleaning. The best part is that when their battery is low, they will return to their docking station to recharge, all on their own. Some models can even be controlled and scheduled using smartphone apps, which is super convenient for those of us with busy schedules.

However, it is not just about convenience. Robotic vacuum cleaners have revolutionized home cleaning, transforming what was once a labor-intensive task into an automated process. They can clean under furniture, navigate around obstacles, and cover large areas, ensuring a thorough cleaning without any human intervention. This is a game-changer, especially for individuals with mobility issues or those with busy schedules.

Personal assistant robots

In the vast tapestry of human history, there have been countless moments that have redefined the way we live. The invention of the wheel, the discovery of electricity, and now, the rise of personal assistant robots. Picture this: a world where a dedicated assistant, always attentive, is just a voice command away. It is not a scene from a sci-fi movie—it is our reality.

Imagine waking up to a gentle voice that reads out your schedule for the day, plays your morning playlist, and even brews your coffee. A world where you are never truly alone, where there is always someone (or rather, something) ready to assist, entertain, or simply chat. It is a world where technology is not just functional but also personal.

Enter the Amazon Echo, a seemingly simple device, but one that has revolutionized our homes. While it might look like just another smart speaker, its essence lies in its capabilities. Powered by Alexa, Amazon's AI assistant, Echo transcends traditional definitions. Sure, it does not have limbs or a face, but its robotic essence is undeniable. It listens, it understands, and it responds. Whether you are multitasking in the kitchen or preparing for a cozy evening, Alexa is there to assist. From setting alarms to controlling room temperature, it is the epitome of convenience.

However, the world of personal assistant robots does not stop at voice commands. Meet Pepper from SoftBank Robotics—a humanoid robot designed to be more than just functional. Pepper is a leap towards the future, a robot that does not just do tasks but also interacts. With its ability to recognize faces, understand emotions, and even engage in conversations, Pepper is blurring the lines between machine and companion. Whether it is greeting customers at a store or dancing to your favorite tune at home, Pepper showcases the potential of robots to be an integral part of our social fabric. You can see Pepper in the following Figure 5.10:

Figure 5.10: Pepper
Source: https://robotsguide.com/robots/pepper)

The rise of personal assistant robots signifies a shift in our relationship with technology. It is not just about automation or efficiency anymore. It is about creating a bond, a semblance of a relationship. These robots bring a blend of utility and companionship, making our interactions with them more organic and less mechanical.

As technology continues to advance, the field of personal assistant robots is set to expand. With innovations in AI and machine learning, these robots will become more intuitive, understanding our needs even before we voice them. As they become more accessible pricewise, it will not be a rare sight to see households across the globe adopting these robotic assistants.

They are more than just toys; they are companions, teachers, and a gateway to a world of creativity and innovation.

It is not just about the playtime, though. Cozmo is designed to engage users in a way that is both fun and meaningful. You can see Cozmo in the following Figure 5.11:

These toys are not just about providing entertainment; they are about shaping the innovators and problem solvers of tomorrow.

These are all the prominent use cases of robotics in today’s world, be it industrial or domestic. Now that we are nearing the end of the chapter, it is time for an exploration of a final (and possibly the one you find the most interesting) aspect of the world of robotics: some of the most popular robots of today!

Famous robots around the world

In this section, we will delve into some of the most groundbreaking and popular robots that have made significant impact across various sectors in the world. From humanoids to advanced machines, these robots are a result of remarkable innovations.

Sophia

Sophia is a humanoid robot from the Hong Kong-based company Hanson Robotics. Activated back on Valentine's Day of 2016, she first appeared before the public eye that year in March in Texas. If you judge her by purpose, Sophia is called a social robot that can essentially mimic human conversations and speech patterns. To underscore her popularity and warm welcome, Sophia was granted citizenship in Saudi Arabia in October 2017, the first of her kind to receive the honor.

Career in robotics

With the rise of automation and AI, robotics is emerging as one of the most promising career paths. Today, robotics is revolutionizing major industries and market sectors and opens a rewarding career with boundless opportunities. Many specialized career fields have emerged as robotics has expanded over the years. Some of them are:

  • Robotics engineering
  • Bio-cybernetics
  • Medical robots
  • Micro-robotics
  • Automation engineering
  • Robotics technicians and operators
  • CAD designer
  • Aerospace engineering, and many more

If you are someone who is enthusiastic about robotics, apart from having a strong background in science, technology, engineering, and mathematics (STEM), you also need to gain hands-on experience with specialized tools and programming languages. Advanced degrees and certifications in areas like machine learning, artificial intelligence, automation, etc., is required to stay ahead in this field. Some popular universities worldwide, like Harvard University, the University of Michigan, Stanford University, the Indian Institute of Technology, and many more, offer top robotics programs.

The future of robotics holds immense potential, offering exciting opportunities for those eager to leverage technology to drive positive change. It presents a gateway for innovation to make a meaningful impact in various industries.

This journey has transformed industries and domestic life and fundamentally altered our understanding of what machines can achieve.

The chapter began by exploring the necessity for robotics, a need rooted in the human desire to automate tasks, enhance efficiency, and augment human capabilities. From the ingenious mechanical devices created by the ancient Greek engineer Heron of Alexandria to the industrial robots that revolutionized manufacturing in the 20th century, the history of robotics is a testament to human ingenuity and the relentless pursuit of progress. This drive to innovate has led to the creation of machines that can replicate, and in some cases surpass, human abilities in various tasks. The field of robotics looks very promising in the coming years and can open up a door of opportunities for enthusiasts who look forward to create a positive impact with technology.

Questions

  1. How do you define robotics? How closely is it tied with artificial intelligence, in your opinion?

    Answer: Robotics is a branch of engineering that involves designing and manufacturing systems known as robots. While AI works to add human-like intelligence to systems, robotics is a completely different branch as it has nothing to do with intelligence. A robot can operate with or without AI depending on its use case.

  2. What were some of the earliest forms of automated machines, and what tasks were they designed to perform?

    Answer: Heron of Alexandria created the first known instance of robotics to perform a variety of tasks with machine-powered automation, including a mechanism to open temple doors automatically.

  3. How has the evolution of robotics technology impacted the manufacturing industry?

    Answer: In manufacturing, robots are involved in assembling, inspection, and packaging. With the advent of robotics, organizations operating in this sector have brought in robots to work alongside human workers, reducing human errors and increasing safety for their human counterparts.

  4. What are some examples of how robotics is used in domestic settings?

    Answer: In our regular houses, robots like Roombas can be brought in to help with cleaning. Further, there can be personal assistant robots, and personal healthcare robots like the fictional character Baymax.

  5. What are some of the challenges that the development and use of robotics present?

    Answer: Robotics brings some ethical issues to be considered. For example, the industries using robots already in their workflow like manufacturing would also be lowering the count of human workers—as robots bring efficiency and nonstop work. This may cause many to lose their jobs, and if they do not upskill in time, the implications become more severe.

  6. How do you think ethical concerns surrounding robotics can be addressed?

    Answer: Those industries employing robots must proactively ensuring they are putting human workers first, and either keep a certain percentage of positions always open for human workers or take the responsibility to upskill their existing employees and create job prospects for them.

  7. Name some of the most popular robots in human history.

    Answer: Some of the popular robots include the social humanoid robot Sophia, and the personal assistant Pepper.

CHAPTER 6AI-driven Automation

Introduction

The last chapter was all about robotics: we discussed its history and use cases and took a look at some of the most famous robots in the world today. Now, while robotics does in part involve automation to achieve most of its use cases, robotics and automation are two completely different technologies, as we have established before.

Compared to the other emerging technologies we discuss in this book, automation is perhaps the one you would be the least interested in, since we have known about its marvels for a while now. However, even if automation is often talked about as a singular technology that essentially takes the burden off human workers across industries, there are nuances there too that might surprise you. For instance, there is the combination of automation and AI that opens up an entirely new avenue of what automation can achieve.

In this chapter, we explore the intricacies of AI-driven automation, its real-world use cases, and the many industries that can benefit from AI-driven automation.

Structure

In this chapter, we will cover the following topics:

  • AI-driven automation
  • AI vs. automation in AI-powered automated systems
  • AI-based automation in blockchain
  • Use cases of AI, automation, and blockchain
  • Applications of a combination of blockchain, AI, and automation

Objective

With this chapter, our goal is to understand automation combined with AI, and how it could magnify the potential of other technologies like blockchain by combining with various aspects of it. By the end of the chapter, we hope you would have a better grasp of how the emerging technologies of today can be combined to serve a wide range of purposes in our steadily digitizing society.

We shall start with the basic definition of what AI-driven automation is.

AI-driven automation

As McKinsey reported in 2018, approximately 30% of the work activities across 60% of the occupations in the past were intended to incorporate automation in the near future. This has proven to be quite true, and with the widespread usage of automation has also come AI. Going by the basic definition, AI-driven automation refers to the use of AI technologies and algorithms to automate a broad variety of tasks, processes, and decision-making in a wide range of industries and applications. Of course, extra emphasis on the decision-making part, since that is the primary benefit of combining AI with automation: you get machinery that can think on their own and complete different tasks without any human intervention whatsoever.

AI-powered automation leverages techniques like ML, deep learning, natural language processing, computer vision, and other streams of AI technologies to make systems and processes more efficient, intelligent, and autonomous.

Components of AI-driven automation

The following are the key components and characteristics of AI-driven automation:

  • Data processing and analysis: AI-driven automation starts with data, as is the case with all AI-powered mechanisms. It involves collecting and analyzing large volumes of data from various sources relevant to the use case to extract valuable insights and patterns. Machine learning models, as we mentioned before, are then trained on this data to perform specific tasks.
  • ML algorithms: ML algorithms are at the core of AI-driven automation. These algorithms enable systems to learn from the collected data and improve their performance over time. Common ML tasks in terms of AI-powered automation include classification, regression, clustering, and recommendation.
  • Decision making: AI-driven automation systems can make decisions or recommendations based on the data they have analyzed, which can be extremely helpful in lowering human intervention in automation procedures. The decisions made by such AI-powered automated systems can range from simple, rule-based choices to complex, probabilistic decisions, depending on the use case.
  • Process automation: AI-driven automation can automate repetitive and rule-based tasks across different domains. For example, in customer service, AI-powered chatbots can handle routine inquiries. In manufacturing, AI-powered robots can automate assembly line processes (as shown in Figure 6.1). As you may have realized, AI-powered automation is indeed already in effect around us.

Figure 6.1: AI-powered automation
Source: https://electrical-engineering-portal.com/9-reasons-for-automation-of-manufacturing-processes

  • Predictive analytics: AI-driven automated systems can predict future events or outcomes based on historical data. This is valuable in various applications, including predictive maintenance in manufacturing and fraud detection in finance.
  • Natural language processing (NLP): NLP technologies enable AI systems to understand and generate human language. This is particularly useful in AI-powered automated systems like chatbots, virtual assistants, and also automated content generation.
  • Computer vision: Computer vision allows AI systems to interpret and understand visual information from images and videos. AI systems enhanced with this feature can combine with automation and be applied in areas like facial recognition, autonomous vehicles, and quality control in manufacturing.
  • Continuous learning: AI-driven automation systems can adapt and improve over time as they are exposed to new data. This adaptability is pretty crucial for staying relevant and accurate in dynamic environments, especially where human intervention might not always be possible.
  • Human-AI collaboration: In many cases, AI-driven automation can be designed to work alongside human operators. A human-robot team can do wonders in terms of enhancing the capabilities of both and improving overall efficiency.
  • Risk management: AI can assess risks and make recommendations to mitigate them, as we know. This can be important in industries like finance, where AI-driven algorithms combined with automation can help manage investment portfolios.

Benefits of AI-driven automation

We already have an idea of what benefits AI-driven automation serve, from the components of it and their varied usability, now let us understand them briefly:

  • AI-driven automation takes care of repetitive tasks and processes, reduces manual effort, and speeds up workflows.
  • AI-powered automated systems can perform tasks with a high degree of precision, reducing the risk of human error.
  • By streamlining operations and reducing labor costs with AI-driven automation, organizations can save money in the long run.
  • Further, AI-driven automated systems can handle increased workloads without a proportional increase in resources.
  • AI can analyze great amounts of data quickly, enabling faster and data-driven decision-making in automated systems.
  • AI-driven analytics can uncover valuable insights from the data gathered too, further aiding in strategic planning.
  • AI-fueled automation can learn and adapt to changing circumstances and new data, staying relevant over time.
  • Organizations that adopt AI-driven automation are bound to gain a competitive edge in their industries.
  • AI-powered automated systems can assist and enhance human capabilities, improving overall performance in a particular organization.
  • Moreover, with AI-driven automation in place, employees can focus on more strategic and creative tasks, as all the routine work is automated.
  • AI-powered automated systems can perform tasks consistently, ensuring uniform quality and compliance with regulations, while being free of errors that usually come with human interference.
  • To have only one area of AI-driven automation as an example, chatbots and virtual assistants can provide 24/7 support, enhancing customer service and satisfaction in retail.
  • To give you another example, in industries like manufacturing, AI-enabled automation can predict equipment failures, reducing downtime and helping plan maintenance costs in advance.
  • AI-driven automation-based recommendation systems can further personalize content and products for users, improving their experience and increasing engagement.
  • In sectors like healthcare and transportation, AI-fueled automated systems can enhance safety through predictive analysis and autonomous systems.
  • AI can assess and mitigate risks in real-time, which makes AI-powered automation particularly useful in areas like financial trading and cybersecurity.

Overall, AI-driven automation possesses the potential to revolutionize various industries by reducing operational costs, increasing productivity, improving decision-making, and enabling brand new and previously impossible capabilities.

AI-driven automation still does raise ethical and societal considerations, such as the impact on employment and privacy of data, which need to be carefully addressed as these technologies continue to evolve. As we have iterated previously as well in the previous section, while using these technologies, it is important to put human needs first, to prevent any misuse of these revolutionary innovations.

Now, before we go further into AI-driven automation, you may still be wondering about automation versus AI. We have discussed these emerging technologies separately beforehand, but in an AI-driven automation system, let us now learn about differentiating between the different components employed, and enhance them further.

AI versus automation in AI-powered automated systems

In the digital era, both artificial intelligence and automation are all set to revolutionize different industries and the way we work today. However, they are of course not synonymous, and in an AI-driven automated system, both bring different characteristics, functionalities, and benefits.

Here is an explanation of the key differences between automation and AI.

Automation

Here is how automation can be explained briefly:

  • Rule-based: Automation involves the use of pre-defined rules and instructions to perform repetitive and structured tasks. An automated system follows a set of predetermined steps or scripts to complete a task.
  • Limited decision-making: Automated systems can make simple decisions based on predefined conditions by themselves, such as if-then statements. It is important to note that these decisions are typically rule-driven and lack adaptability to new or unforeseen situations.
  • No learning capability: Automation systems themselves do not possess the ability to learn from data or experiences. They perform tasks exactly as programmed, without the capacity to improve or adapt over time.

Examples of automation

Robotic Process Automation (RPA) is a common form of automation where software-based robots perform repetitive, instruction-based tasks like data entry, form filling, and file transfers.

Automated conveyor belts in manufacturing can be another example of automation technology.

Artificial intelligence

Now, here is how we can sum up artificial intelligence:

  • Adaptive and intelligent: AI systems are designed to mimic human intelligence by processing data, recognizing patterns, and making decisions. They can handle both routine and complex tasks.
  • Learning capability: AI involves learning from data and experiences through ML algorithms. This allows AI systems to improve their performance, accuracy, and decision-making over time.
  • Advanced decision-making: AI can make complex decisions by considering multiple factors and probabilistic outcomes. These systems can adapt to changing conditions and make decisions beyond predefined rules, on par with humans, if not better than them.

Examples of AI

Virtual assistants like Siri and Alexa (Figure 6.2) use NLP to understand and respond to voice commands, as AI-powered systems:

Figure 6.2: Amazon’s Alexa is an example of an AI-powered system
Source: https://www.wired.com/2017/02/amazon-alexa-hits-10000-skills-plenty-room-grow/

Autonomous vehicles also use AI to navigate and make real-time decisions based on sensor data.

Automation versus AI

Here are the features of these technologies stacked up against each other so you can summarize which part can be attributed to which technology in an AI-powered automated system. Let us take a look at automation versus AI with the help of the following table:

Attributes

AI

Automation

Manual intervention

AI possesses human-like intelligence relevant to its area of operation, requiring little to no human intervention.

Automation can only perform pre-programmed tasks, requiring human intervention with a change in environment.

Adaptability and intelligence

AI is more intelligent, adaptable, and capable of learning from data.

Automation is rule-driven and lacks adaptability.

Complexity of tasks to handle

AI can handle tasks that are too complex for traditional automation, and it has the potential to make decisions and predictions based on data analysis.

Automation simply follows predefined instructions and requires further programming when working conditions are changed.

Focus area

AI is mainly focused on decision-making capabilities based on data and insights fed to it.

Automation focuses on precise, repetitive tasks to free up human workers from tireless, time-consuming activities.

Table 6.1: Automation versus AI

Now, AI and automation are usually combined to create sophisticated systems that benefit from both AI-driven intelligence and rule-based automation across industries. AI is quite new to everyday operations, while automation has been in use for years, especially in areas such as manufacturing.

Examples of AI-powered automation

AI-driven automation can be applied across various industries and domains to streamline processes, enhance efficiency, and improve decision-making. Some examples include:

  • Autocorrect and text editors: Starting with an example that would remind you that we indeed do use AI-based automation in our daily lives without realizing it. In word processors, texting applications, and any digital medium of writing, we use AI algorithms to identify our errors and automatically correct them. This is facilitated by AI-based automated systems using ML, deep learning, and NLP or natural language processing.
  • Manufacturing: In manufacturing, AI-powered robots can automate tasks like assembly, welding, and quality control. These intelligent robots can adapt to changes in the production line and work alongside human workers to increase efficiency.
  • Healthcare: AI algorithms can analyze medical images, such as X-rays and MRIs, to detect abnormalities and assist in diagnosis. Further, chatbots and virtual health assistants powered by AI can provide patient information, schedule appointments, and answer common healthcare questions.
  • Customer service: AI-driven chatbots are already used in customer support to handle routine inquiries and provide 24/7 support.
  • Assistants: Further, AI-based voice assistants, like Amazon’s Alexa and the Google Assistant, can perform tasks like setting reminders, answering questions, and controlling smart home devices.
  • Searching algorithms for personalization: When you go to search for a movie on a streaming service or navigate through an e-commerce store, you may have noticed that the recommendations match your taste. This is brought on by an AI-powered algorithm automatically monitoring your online activities to analyze your preferences. The data collected from your activities is fed to a machine learning and deep learning process so the system can predict which items would fit your tastes next.
  • Finance: AI automated algorithms can analyze market data and execute trades for you, making split-second decisions based upon changes in market conditions.
  • E-transactions: Speaking of finance, the fact that we can make payments to businesses from the convenience of our homes is brought on by banks using AI to streamline payments for us. AI-based automation can be used to ensure security, identity verification, privacy, and more, while we make payments and share our account details to receive e-payments ourselves.
  • Fraud detection: AI-based automated systems can monitor exchanges of personal and financial data in real-time and on a routine basis to identify and prevent fraudulent activities. In fact, AI algorithms can track your spending patterns and also keep watch on what kind of things you frequently purchase, and which are your go-to shops, so they can detect any unusual activities using your financial information.
  • Retail: AI automation can help retailers optimize inventory levels by analyzing demand patterns, reducing overstocking, and preventing stockouts. What is more, AI-fueled personalized services can suggest products to customers based on their browsing and purchase history.
  • Autonomous vehicles: Self-driving cars and trucks can use AI-based automation for navigation, obstacle detection, and decision-making in real-time.
  • Logistics: Along with facilitating ease and efficiency in autonomous vehicles, AI and automation-based systems can optimize delivery routes for logistics companies, reducing fuel consumption and delivery times.
  • Agriculture: AI automation can help farmers by analyzing data from sensors and drones to optimize planting, irrigation, and harvesting processes.
  • Energy management: AI can be used to manage and optimize energy distribution in smart grids, thus balancing supply and demand more evenly and fairly, and reducing wastage of valuable energy.
  • Recruitment: AI-based automation can also assist you in screening job applicants, by matching candidates to job descriptions, and even conducting initial interviews.
  • Blockchain: AI-based automation can be a driving force when it comes to maintaining security in blockchains. AI can further streamline blockchain transactions, enhance smart contracts with better automation combined with intelligence, aside from the enhanced security and privacy.

All these examples tell us that AI-based automation is a versatile technology that you can create for specific focus areas across virtually any industry you can think of. AI-automation leads to numerous benefits like increased efficiency, saving up on expenses, and of course, precise and improved decision-making. Both emerging technologies stand to play a pivotal role in transforming how businesses and organizations operate across multiple sectors.

As you may have noted, we have discussed the usage of AI-based automation in relation to blockchain; another emerging technology highlighted in this book. It is time to explore into that region and understand why AI-based automation and blockchain can be a stellar combination.

AI-based automation in blockchain

As we have previously seen, the intersection of AI and blockchain can potentially transform a wide number of industries. Now, AI-driven automation enables machines to assist humans with a broad variety of jobs and make decisions, while blockchain provides secure, transparent, and tamper-proof applications. The convergence of AI, automation, and blockchain thus holds the potential to revolutionize several industries by enhancing security, transparency, and efficiency.

For instance, as we were discussing, in cybersecurity, AI and automation can detect and respond to threats, while blockchain ensures data integrity and security. This combination can lead to excellently robust cybersecurity systems, eradicating most of the issues we face today due to big data companies holding a monopoly over our data.

Financial services can also benefit from a combination of AI, automation, and blockchain by enhancing security and efficiency in payment systems, including fraud detection and secure transactions. Additionally, decentralized marketplaces also leverage AI and automation for personalized recommendations, while blockchain is there for authenticity and provenance verification.

To sum up, the synergy of AI and blockchain is poised to transform various industries. Which is precisely why we will explore the nuances of AI-based automation in blockchain in the next section.

Combining blockchain and AI-driven automation

There are several compelling reasons as to why you should want to combine all three of these emerging technologies. The benefits include enhanced authenticity and enhancement of operations all around.

Firstly, the transparency and immutability of blockchain allows for the validation of AI processes and data ownership. Therefore, when the two are combined, data integrity concerns attached to AI are done away with. We can say that blockchain serves as a secure repository for AI models, creating an audit trail that bolsters data security and trust.

AI-powered automation also has the ability to swiftly analyze data and identify patterns that align perfectly with blockchain's data repository capabilities. Together, they introduce a new level of intelligence to blockchain networks. This augmentation enables organizations to access and derive actionable insights from vast data sources, both internal and external.

Finally, the synergy of blockchain, AI, and automation excels in streamlining complex business processes involving multiple stakeholders. AI within smart contracts, for instance, can automate actions based on specific conditions, streamlining operations, enhancing efficiency, and reducing friction. This can be particularly beneficial in areas like supply chain management and dispute resolution.

In essence, the combination of blockchain, automation, and AI empowers organizations to enhance authenticity, streamline operations, and tap into augmented intelligence for improved decision-making and efficiency. This synergy holds the potential to revolutionize various industries by leveraging the strengths of both technologies.

Now, let us see how well the combination of AI, blockchain, and automation works in three areas in particular: transactions, smart contracts, and the security aspect of blockchains.

Streamlining blockchain transactions with AI and automation

AI and automation can function as powerful tools to streamline and enhance blockchain transactions. Blockchain technology, of course, is known for its security and transparency, and it has already revolutionized industries such as finance, supply chain, and healthcare. However, the integration of AI and automation takes the capabilities of blockchain to the next level by:

  • Improving efficiency
  • Reducing costs
  • Increasing the scalability of blockchain networks

Here is how AI and automation can be leveraged to streamline blockchain transactions:

  • Smart contracts: Smart contracts are self-executing agreements with the terms of the contract agreed upon by all counterparties directly written into code. AI can be used to optimize and automate the execution of these contracts based on predefined conditions. This not only ensures that agreements are automatically enforced, but also reduces the need for intermediaries, making transactions faster and more cost-effective, not to mention more secure as well.
  • Scalability: Blockchain networks often face challenges related to scalability as the number of transactions increases. AI can assist in optimizing network performance by predicting traffic patterns, ensuring that transactions are processed efficiently even during peak times.
  • Predictive analytics: AI algorithms can analyze historical transaction data on the blockchain to predict future trends (such as regarding trading volumes after analyzing user behavior around certain times of the year like the festive season or any macroeconomic conditions) and potential issues (such as those related to scalability). This predictive analysis can be used to optimize transaction routing, prevent fraud, and ensure the smooth flow of payments.
  • Enhanced security: AI-powered security systems can be automated to continuously monitor blockchain networks for anomalies and potential threats. If any suspicious activity is detected, AI can trigger automated responses or alerts, allowing for rapid threat mitigation and preserving the integrity of the blockchain.
  • Transaction verification: AI algorithms can assist in verifying the authenticity of transactions, ensuring that only valid transactions are added to the blockchain. This helps in reducing the risk of fraudulent or erroneous transactions. However, this does raise some questions regarding the role of validators on blockchains, and it falls on developers to ensure that AI-driven automation being placed is not imposing upon the human factor in blockchain activities.
  • NLP: NLP-powered chatbots and virtual assistants can facilitate user interactions with blockchain networks. Users can query the blockchain, initiate transactions, or receive updates on their accounts through natural language, making blockchain more accessible to a wider audience, especially the non-tech savvy kind.
  • Compliance and reporting: Automation tools can ensure that blockchain transactions are compliant with evolving regulatory requirements by automatically generating reports and maintaining transaction records. This reduces the burden of compliance management for organizations.

The combination of AI and automation with blockchain technology stands to transform the way transactions are conducted on blockchains. By automating various aspects of blockchain operations and using AI to optimize performance, organizations can streamline their blockchain transactions, making chains more efficient, secure, and scalable.

Scalability is one of the key issues blockchains face today, standing as the biggest obstacle on blockchain’s way to widespread adoption. However, if intelligent automated systems can streamline operations during peak hours and reroute transactions across layer one and layer two solutions, that obstacle is eliminated efficiently.

Of course, increased scalability is only one of the many reasons why the combination of AI and automation is magnificent in streamlining blockchain transactions, as we have seen in Streamlining Blockchain transactions with AI and automation. The synergy of AI and automation is no doubt poised to play a crucial role in the widespread adoption of blockchain technology across various industries.

AI-based automation for smart contracts

You already have an idea of how artificial intelligence enhances smart contract functionality in blockchain. AI and automation are emerging as powerful enablers for the creation and execution of smart contracts, offering greater efficiency, accuracy, and adaptability to this cornerstone of blockchain technology the Ethereum blockchain first brought about.

In case the definition escapes you, smart contracts are of course pieces of self-executing digital agreements that come with predefined rules and conditions from all counterparties involved in a transaction. There is already a touch of automation in smart contracts, as you can evidently see.

Smart contracts are built on blockchain platforms like Ethereum and others that support smart contract functionality. To make finance truly decentralized on blockchains, the smart contract is one of the mechanisms giving power over their finances back to the sender and receiver in said contract.

Now, coming to the topic at hand, here are some ways how AI and automation are enhancing smart contracts:

  • Automated contract creation: AI can assist in drafting and generating smart contracts by analyzing natural language inputs from users. This simplifies the contract creation process and reduces the need for users to write complex code, making smart contracts more accessible, again, especially to the non-tech savvy.
  • NLP: NLP algorithms can interpret and convert human-readable contract terms into machine-readable code. This bridging of the human-computer language gap simplifies the creation of smart contract templates.
  • Automated validation: AI-driven tools can automatically validate the conditions and clauses in smart contracts. This ensures that the code adheres to the intended logic and minimizes the risk of errors.
  • Monitoring and compliance: AI-driven monitoring tools can track real-time data relevant to a contract's conditions. If a condition is met or violated, the smart contract can automatically execute actions, such as transferring funds or sending notifications.
  • Dynamic contract execution: AI enables smart contracts to adapt and respond to real-world events and data. For instance, smart contracts at work in the insurance domain can use weather data to automatically trigger payouts in case of adverse weather conditions.
  • Risk assessment: AI algorithms can assess the risk associated with various clauses and conditions in smart contracts. This helps users make informed decisions about contract terms and negotiate better agreements.
  • Predictive analytics: AI can analyze historical data and optimize contract terms and conditions in keeping with upcoming market trends and patterns, leading to more efficient and fair agreements.
  • Automated escrow services: In complex multi-signature contracts, AI can automate the escrow process, ensuring that funds or assets are held securely until predefined conditions are met. This reduces the need for intermediaries.
  • Secure Oracles: Oracles are third-party services that provide data to blockchain-based smart contracts. AI can enhance the security of oracles by verifying data authenticity and ensuring the reliability of external data sources.
  • Dispute resolution: AI can provide dispute resolution mechanisms within smart contracts. If parties disagree, the AI can analyze contract terms and transaction data to propose further fair resolutions.

Smart contracts are an important component in ensuring smooth blockchain transactions and maintaining decentralization in trades with fair terms for all parties involved. By simplifying and enhancing smart contract creation and magnifying both the aforementioned components in turn, AI-driven automation offers robust and versatile solutions for a wide range of industries.

AI and automation for security in blockchain

Finally, the role of AI in enhancing the security factor of blockchains. Now, we know blockchains are all about the decentralization of finance and data-sharing in general. This is also why blockchains allow a certain degree of privacy for its participants, so people can send transactions by authenticating their private keys and without revealing their true identities.

Along with ensuring the anonymity of participants, blockchain also keeps transactions transparent to authorized users and immutable, so no bad actor can tamper with the records without the rest of the chain also knowing. All that we already know, but still there are chances of tampering happening on-chain from time to time when enough decentralization is not achieved, resulting in significant losses for users. AI-driven automation can help here.

Artificial intelligence and automation have the potential to play a pivotal role in enhancing security and privacy within blockchain technology. AI can further fortify the security aspects of blockchains, making the networks more robust and privacy-respecting, in the following ways:

  • Enhanced anomaly detection: AI-driven automated algorithms can continuously monitor blockchain networks for unusual activities or anomalies. Any unexpected behavior or suspicious transactions can trigger alerts for further investigation, reducing the risk of security breaches.
  • Historical data analysis and pattern recognition: AI can analyze historical transaction patterns and identify potential threats. It can automatically recognize recurring patterns associated with fraudulent activities, enabling early intervention and fraud prevention.
  • Privacy-preserving techniques: AI can be used to develop privacy-preserving techniques, such as zero-knowledge proofs, to ensure transactions that allow users to transact on blockchain while keeping their data private and secure.
  • Secure multi-party computation: AI can facilitate secure multi-party computation, enabling multiple parties to jointly compute a function over their inputs while keeping those inputs private. This is valuable for privacy-preserving applications.
  • Smart contract auditing: AI can automatically audit smart contracts for vulnerabilities and potential exploits. This ensures that smart contracts are secure from the outset, reducing the risk of code-based weaknesses and attacks.
  • Identity verification: AI-powered identity verification systems can be integrated with blockchain networks to enhance user authentication and authorization. This reduces the risk of unauthorized access and identity theft.
  • Secure data sharing: AI can enable secure data sharing on blockchain networks. It allows users to grant access only to authorized parties while maintaining data privacy.
  • Encryption and decryption: AI algorithms can enhance encryption techniques used in blockchain networks, making it even more challenging for unauthorized entities to access sensitive data stored on the blockchain.
  • Behavioral analysis: AI can perform behavioral analysis of network participants to detect unusual or malicious behavior. This includes tracking transaction histories and verifying if users' actions align with their usual patterns.
  • Predictive threat intelligence: AI can gather threat intelligence from various sources, predict potential security threats to blockchain networks, and stay alert against those. This proactive approach helps in preemptively safeguarding the network.
  • Privacy tokens: AI can be used to develop privacy-focused crypto tokens that prioritize user anonymity while ensuring transaction validity.

By integrating AI and automation into blockchain networks, users and organizations can benefit from heightened security and privacy protections. Combined technologies can not only mitigate existing threats but also proactively anticipate and prevent emerging security issues.

Going by all three of the use cases of AI and automation in blockchain we just discussed, the future certainly holds great promise for more secure, private, and resilient blockchain ecosystems. Meanwhile, both AI and automation are consistently evolving technologies, which means the future holds brand new applications of these combinations that we have yet to anticipate. It sure is going to be an exhilarating ride!

Use cases of AI, automation, and blockchain

Now let us take a look at some use cases of the convergence of AI, automation, and blockchain technology. While some of these may already be in practice, others are exploring future possibilities:

Supply chain management

Blockchain provides an immutable ledger for tracking the movement of goods in the supply chain, ensuring transparency and traceability. AI then analyzes the collected data to optimize inventory management and predict supply chain disruptions. Further, automation through smart contracts can automate and streamline procurement, payments, and quality control processes, reducing manual intervention and improving efficiency.

In a relevant stream, for food safety, blockchain can track the origin and journey of food products. AI can then analyze this data to detect contamination or spoilage issues and sound alarms and halt supplies of a particular item when necessary.

Healthcare

Blockchain can securely store and manage patient records, ensuring data integrity and privacy. AI can analyze the collected data for medical diagnosis, treatment recommendations, and predictive analytics.

Moreover, blockchain can help track the production and distribution of pharmaceuticals, while AI can assist in identifying any counterfeit drugs and ensuring patient safety.

Financial services

Blockchain facilitates faster and cheaper cross-border transactions. AI can meanwhile enhance fraud detection and automate compliance checks for these transactions. AI-powered algorithms also can execute high-frequency trading strategies on blockchain-based cryptocurrency exchanges, optimizing trading decisions. This later use case is one already being used across trading bots.

Energy sector

We already know that blockchain can enable peer-to-peer energy trading among households with renewable energy sources. Here, AI can predict energy production and consumption, optimizing trading decisions. Further, AI and blockchain can automate grid management, ensuring efficient energy distribution and reducing waste.

Law

Blockchain can automate and self-execute legal contracts based on predefined conditions, while AI can review and analyze legal documents for compliance and accuracy. Relevantly, blockchain can also be used to track and protect intellectual property rights, while AI can search for patent infringements and copyright violations.

Real estate

Blockchain simplifies property transactions by securely recording and transferring ownership. AI can in the meantime assess property values, automate due diligence, and predict real estate market trends for maximized efficiency in the sector.

Education and job market

Blockchain can store and verify educational credentials, reducing fraud and simplifying the hiring process for companies. Further, AI can personalize educational content and recommend courses based on individual learning patterns and the industries one wants to work in.

Government and elections

Blockchain ensures the integrity and security of electronic voting systems. AI can contribute here too by providing real-time monitoring for fraudulent activities and automated vote counting.

Internet of Things

Finally, with the age of the Internet of Things (IoT) rising, blockchain can secure IoT device identities and transactions, while AI can analyze IoT data to optimize device performance and predict maintenance needs. Further, automation is of course one core aspect of IoT devices.

All these use cases illustrate the diverse applications of AI, automation, and blockchain technology, especially when brought together. By combining these technologies, organizations can improve efficiency, transparency, and security across various sectors, ultimately leading to more innovative and streamlined processes.

Now it is time for a quick look at the real-life applications of a combination of blockchain, AI, and automation technologies.

Applications of a combination of blockchain, AI, and automation

The tools made available by the company automate workflow in the construction industry, not to mention increase the speed at which they operate. Further, the technology put out by FINALIZE can also be fit into wearables to ensure they meet all safety requirements.
  • BLACKBOX AI: While not directly related, this initiative's objective is to create tailored artificial intelligence solutions for emerging technologies. The team specializes in building customized information frameworks that support a range of applications, including ML, NLP, and blockchain tools. The company already has experience with renowned tech giants like Apple, Intel, and NVIDIA, having developed AI-based tools for diverse applications, including virtual reality and natural language processing.

Automation does not bring intelligence to the table, lacks adaptability, cannot handle complex tasks without reprogramming, and is best at precise and repetitive tasks.
