CHAPTER 7Convergence of AI and Blockchain

Introduction

In the last chapter, we learned about AI-driven automation: from the definition to the objectives of this stellar combination. Further, we discussed some use cases of AI-driven automation and a couple of real-life examples of the same. If you recall, we also took a brief look at AI-driven automation in association with blockchain in the previous chapter. Now, it is time to discuss the convergence of AI and blockchain in particular.

AI and blockchain are two of the most popular emerging technologies of our day and age. We will now see what happens when we combine these two technologies together. While this book has previously discussed the potential of this combination in bits and pieces, it is time to get into it fully. Without further ado, let us get started in our understanding of the interwoven possibilities of AI and blockchain!

Structure

This chapter covers the following topics:

  • AI and core technologies
  • Strengths and weaknesses of AI
  • Blockchain and core technologies
  • Strengths and weaknesses of blockchain
  • Use cases of AI and blockchain
  • Benefits of using AI in decentralized applications
  • Examples of AI-blockchain convergence

Objectives

As mentioned, our mission this time is to learn what AI and blockchain are capable of doing together. We will take a look at the basics of both technologies, along with their respective strengths and weaknesses. Then we will proceed to look at how the two combine for the greater good of humanity, and what exciting use cases they produce. Finally, we analyze the benefits of putting AI and decentralized applications together, as well as some real-world examples of the combination. Stay tuned!

AI and core technologies

Let us start with a quick callback to the basics of AI and blockchain before we go into understanding the potential of AI and blockchain together.

Defining AI

AI refers to an emulation of human intelligence in machines; therefore, it is essentially the science of programming machines to think and learn like humans.

Goal of AI

The goal of AI is to develop systems and technologies which can perform tasks on par with human beings, especially the kind that requires human intelligence. The particular genre of tasks AI aims to be involved in include problem-solving, understanding natural language, recognizing speech and thought patterns, and most importantly: making decisions.

Core technologies of AI

There are several technologies that make up an AI system, these core technologies involved in AI include:

Machine learning

Machine learning (ML) is a subset of AI that focuses on enabling machines to continuously learn from acquired data without being explicitly programmed to do so. ML involves creating algorithms which can improve their performance on a task through experience and data analysis. Some common ML techniques include regression, classification, clustering, and deep learning.

Deep learning

Deep learning is a certain form of ML which uses artificial neural networks inspired by the structure of the human brain. These neural networks (deep neural networks in particular) can analyze vast amounts of data and are widely used in image and speech recognition, natural language processing, and recommendation systems.

Natural language processing

Natural language processing (NLP) is a branch of AI that smoothens the interaction between AI systems and human language. NLP enables machines to not only understand and interpret, but also speak in human language. As you can guess, NLP is in wide use in applications like chatbots and language translation, as well as sentiment analysis for AI systems.

Computer vision

This component of AI systems involves teaching computers to interpret and understand visual information from the world, such as images and videos. Computer vision is used in applications like object detection, facial recognition, autonomous vehicles, and medical image analysis.

Speech recognition

You might be quite familiar with this technology due to assistant AI devices like Amazon’s Alexa inhabiting almost every home these days. Speech recognition technology allows machines to convert spoken language into text and vice versa. It is used in applications like voice assistants, transcription services, and interactive voice response systems.

AI hardware

Specialized hardware, such as Graphics Processing Units (GPUs) and Tensor Processing Units (TPUs), is designed to accelerate artificial intelligence and deep learning tasks. These components are crucial for the high computational demands of AI applications.

AI software development tools

There are numerous software tools, libraries and frameworks, such as TensorFlow, PyTorch, and scikit-learn, that make it easier for developers to build and train AI models.

Generative AI

ChatGPT has made this term very popular today. Generative AI models make use of completely unsupervised or partially supervised machine learning algorithms, and they enable computers to use text, audio, video, code, images, and other input fed to it to generate fresh content akin to the input. Thus, generative AI models learn the features and relations of an input and can generate output that is unique from the training dataset.

There are Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) that can use text and images as input forms to generate multimedia content. On the other hand, there are transformer-based generative AI models that can use data available on the internet to create new textual content.

Robotics

Robotics in combination with AI can create autonomous or semi-autonomous robots that can perform tasks like assembly, exploration, and even surgery. Robotics can also help create highly human-like, sociable robots that can keep people company and engage in conversations on anything and everything!

Reinforcement learning

Reinforcement learning is one type of machine learning where a system learns to make decisions by interacting with an environment. Technology uses a chain of rewards and punishments to guide this learning process for the system. Reinforcement learning is used most in areas like robotics.

Expert systems

Expert systems are AI programs that are built to emulate the decision-making abilities of human experts in their areas of operation. These systems use technological solutions like knowledge bases and inference engines to propose recommendations or solve problems in certain scenarios.

Strengths and weaknesses of AI

When combined with blockchain for the greater good of humanity, AI systems possess both strengths and weaknesses. On the one hand, there is the heightened efficiency factor, but on the other, you have ethical issues to consider.

Strengths of AI in building the future

We already know the many benefits of AI, but here is a quick look at the strengths:

  • AI can automate repetitive tasks, reducing human labor and errors.
  • AI across industries can lead to increased productivity and cost savings in a wide range of tasks. In fact, there are very few tasks that AI cannot perform.
  • AI can allow humans to focus more on creative tasks by automating the less than pleasant part of their works.
  • AI can enhance accessibility for individuals with disabilities.
  • AI systems can make technology and information more widely available to all. Further, AI-driven educational tools can provide personalized learning experiences, making education more effective and accessible to a broader audience, helping them learn at their own pace.
  • In healthcare, AI can assist in medical diagnosis, drug discovery, and personalized treatment plans, improving patient outcomes and saving lives.
  • AI can optimize resource management and energy consumption, contributing to more sustainable and eco-friendly practices.
  • Artificial intelligence can accelerate research and data analysis, leading to breakthroughs in various fields, from climate science to genomics.
  • AI can drive economic growth by fostering innovation and creating whole new industries in turn.

Weaknesses of AI to address in the future

Away from all the positives- which admittedly are numerous, here are some disadvantages of AI that would need to be addressed before the usage of AI tools becomes more widespread:

  • Automation by AI can lead to job displacement in certain industries, potentially causing economic and social disruption. Our target while utilizing technological solutions should be to put the human factor first, and therefore the issue of job displacement must be actively solved by those of us choosing to use AI solutions in our organizations.
  • Coming to a more technological flaw, AI algorithms can inherit biases from training data, which could lead to discriminatory outcomes. We do not want this happening in areas like healthcare, financial lending, or new recruitments, which is why this issue should be proactively solved while training an AI system.
  • AI's data-driven nature can raise privacy issues when sensitive personal information is collected and shared, such as to create datasets to feed AI systems. This is another factor to watch out for on the end of developers.
  • The training of larger AI models consumes a significant amount of energy, which can create severe environmental concerns. The use of renewable energy sources is recommended for the same reason.
  • Since AI is just starting out as a widespread technology, the benefits of AI are not yet fairly distributed. This might potentially exacerbate the issue of economic inequality initially, which is again something to actively work on.
  • AI systems may be exploited to carry out cyberattacks. For example, an autonomous car may suddenly stop following the orders, or worse, it may plan an accident. This is yet another facet where developers would have to forge solutions to secure the future of mankind.
  • Another issue of AI would be the over-reliance on it by brands, healthcare professionals, and more, which can lead to a reduction of the human connection at the least and handing out wrong information at the worst; this can be particularly deadly in healthcare. There would always be a certain level of human involvement necessary, especially in the more sensitive industries.

In areas like warfare, autonomous decision making can prove deadly. AI does pose several ethical challenges like this, especially when there is a lack of accountability and transparency. It is important to make the decision-making processes of AI systems clear to even the non-tech savvy, and actively engage in raising transparency and accepting accountability for those engaging in the creation of AI models for various purposes.

Overall, to harness the strengths of AI for the broader good of humanity while mitigating its weaknesses, it is essential to:

  • Adopt responsible AI practices
  • Invest in widespread AI education
  • Establish ethical frameworks
  • Ensure that the benefits of AI are accessible to a wide range of people from all walks of life

As we have mentioned several times, ethical considerations, regulation, and ongoing conversations among stakeholders (and adherence to said conversations) are critical in shaping the future of AI for the betterment of society.

Now, we will explore the second technology discussed in this chapter. The fundamentals of blockchain, along with its strengths and weaknesses, significantly impact the future of mankind. Let us look.

Blockchain and core technologies

Let us begin by defining blockchain, its goal and the core technologies behind it.

Defining blockchain

If you would remember our very first chapter on decentralized technologies, blockchain is itself a distributed ledger technology that allows a secure and transparent recording of transactions across a network of computers called nodes, spread all around the world.

Goal of blockchain

Blockchain was born alongside Bitcoin as the underlying technology for cryptocurrencies. However, its applications have since expanded beyond just digital assets. Blockchain is being used around the globe for real-world use cases, such as supply chain management, agricultural welfare, personal loans and other centralized financial services, and more.

Core technologies of blockchain

Just to refresh our memory, the core technologies and concepts involved in blockchain are as follows.

Distributed ledger

At its core, a blockchain is a distributed ledger which maintains a record of transactions across a worldwide network of nodes. There is no central authority figure to maintain the ledger, instead the network of nodes spread out around the globe collectively maintain it.

Nodes

Since a blockchain is decentralized, the power to make decisions relies on every participant on the network, and everyone gets an equal say. The computers connected to a blockchain network from all around the world are called nodes, which network participants can use to connect to the network and participate in decision making.

Blocks

Transactions on a blockchain are grouped together into virtual blocks, and each of these blocks contains a list of validated transactions, timestamped to maintain uniform records. Each block on the chain is linked to its previous block with a hash that contains a component of the previous block’s hash, and therefore the records are immutable and tamper-proof.

Cryptography

A blockchain relies on cryptographic techniques to secure all transactions and prevent bad actors from messing with the records being kept. Public and private keys are provided to users which they can use to authenticate and authorize transactions, as well as receive transactions. Overall, cryptography ensures the confidentiality and integrity of blockchain transactions, protecting users’ personal and financial information.

Cryptocurrency

Most blockchains have their own native cryptocurrencies, like Bitcoin’s BTC or Ethereum’s ETH. These native cryptocurrencies are often used to incentivize network participants to participate in block validation, pay transaction fees, and enable value transfer between blockchain nodes.

Consensus mechanisms

To maintain the integrity of the distributed ledger, all blockchain networks employ consensus mechanisms that help all nodes agree on how transactions are validated and added to the ledger. Each node is thus sure to get an equal say. Common consensus mechanisms used by blockchains today include Proof of Work (PoW) (for example, Bitcoin), Proof of Stake (PoS) (For example, Ethereum), and Delegated Proof of Stake (DPoS) (for example, Tron). Yet more consensus mechanisms for blockchains are highlighted in Figure 7.1:

Figure 7.1: Different blockchain consensus mechanisms
Source: https://medium.com/coinmonks/blockchain-blog-15-blockchain-consensus-protocols-ac07d35bce4c

Smart contracts

Smart contracts, conceived as we know them today in 2015 by the Ethereum blockchain, are self-executing agreements with predefined rules and conditions. These contracts are executed once all predetermined conditions by the counterparties involved are met. Almost all blockchains created after Ethereum have or aim to have this facility, so as to ensure truly decentralized processes.

Strengths and weaknesses of blockchain

Just like AI, blockchain technology offers several strengths and advantages that can benefit humanity, but it also comes with its share of weaknesses and challenges. Here are some of the strengths and weaknesses of blockchain for humanity, starting with the notable advantages.

Strengths of blockchain in building the future

Blockchain technology has many strengths, and due to lesser moral considerations as compared to AI, you could say blockchain is probably one step closer to achieving widespread adoption among the global populace.

Here are the strength factors of blockchain:

  • Due to its decentralized nature and ability to build direct peer-to-peer connections, blockchain can completely do away with all intermediaries. This helps you reduce expenses and increase efficiency in various processes, such as fund transfers and banking (especially cross-border transfers) and supply chain management.
  • With its decentralization factor, blockchain can increase the scope of financial inclusion and get essential services to the doors of the yet unbanked and underbanked. With blockchain and decentralized financial applications, anyone with a semi-decent internet connection can avail financial services with a click.
  • Blockchain's decentralized and immutable ledger makes it highly secure and resistant to tampering, which can be instrumental in various applications. Further, it makes any wrongdoings—whether intentional or unintentional—immediately apparent to all network participants.
  • Self-executing smart contracts on blockchain can automate all agreements and transactions, reducing the need for manual processes and associated errors.
  • Transactions on a public blockchain are transparent and traceable to all, enhancing accountability and reducing fraud. With this factor, you can begin to see how blockchain can be a solution to some of the ethical considerations related to AI.
  • Blockchains give power over their own information and finances back to the user. Therefore, all individuals can have better control over their private data and digitized/digital assets, reducing data breaches and privacy violations. Again, a combination of AI and blockchain can thus be helpful to ensure no private data is being fed to an artificial intelligence system.
  • Once data is recorded on the blockchain, it cannot be altered or deleted, ensuring data integrity, which is particularly crucial for usage in medical record-keeping and supply chains.
  • Blockchain reduces the dependence on centralized authorities for all parties involved, promoting more distributed and democratic systems. This is one of the core benefits of any decentralized model.

Weaknesses of blockchain to address in the future

Now, let us get into the disadvantages of blockchain:

  • Quite a few blockchain platforms of today face scalability challenges, limiting the number of transactions they can process. This is especially common in the older blockchains like Bitcoin. Ethereum, which also faced severe scalability issues, has recently transitioned to a proof of stake consensus algorithm, as we know, and the new chain will put measures in place to address the challenges related to scalability.
  • A lack of scalability can cause many issues like delays in transaction processing, high fees, or complete breakdown of the system from overload. One of the key obstructions on blockchain’s way to the mainstream, the scalability challenges must be addressed before the age of decentralization can truly begin.

  • Proof of work blockchains like Bitcoin require significant energy for the mining operations, which is not sustainable in the long run due to environmental concerns. This was another reason for Ethereum to switch to proof of stake.

    After the merger in 2022, the new Ethereum blockchain has been able to reduce its energy consumption by over 99%, a feat that should be an example for PoW blockchains looking to survive in the long term.

  • As is true for any new technology, blockchains can be complex and challenging to implement with existing infrastructure across industries initially. This is especially true for non-tech savvy organizations, and the onboarding costs can also be harsh.
  • Further, the regulation of blockchains is intricately tied to that of crypto to some extent, which may bring regulatory uncertainty surrounding blockchain in some countries and regions. Overall, the regulatory environment for blockchain is still evolving, which might lead to uncertainties and potential legal challenges.
  • In some cases, complete decentralization may lead to governance problems, such as decision-making conflicts. Such disagreements can lead to hard forks in blockchains and in such cases, the governance difficulties may be long lasting.
  • The immutability of blockchains can, on rare occasions, prove to be a weakness when there may be record keeping errors or fraudulent transactions to reverse.
  • Blockchains can fall prey to some specific cyber-attacks, especially those with smaller networks. For instance, there could be a 51% attack, where an attacker teams up with over 50% of the network to cause disruptions in the network’s usual procedures. Such difficulties can be hard to sort out.
  • Blockchains offer complete data transparency, which can again prove to be disadvantageous in certain cases. For example, in healthcare, privacy is an essential component, which blockchain might not allow for. However, usually these issues in particular can be solved by simply choosing a private network instead of a public one.

Overall, scalability issues are the biggest dilemma blockchains of today are facing, and a comprehensive solution is yet to emerge. While developers continue to work on that, blockchain definitely is taking over the mainstream despite multiple difficulties.

To harness the strengths of blockchain for the betterment of humanity and mitigate its weaknesses, responsible and thoughtful implementation of the technology is essential, as is true with AI as well. The fact remains that blockchain is a technology that can be put to work for the greater good, and it can be used effectively to address real-world challenges and opportunities.

Now that we are clear on the key strengths and weaknesses of both AI and blockchain, let us take a look at some practical use cases and how combining these strengths will ensure a technologically secured and bright future for humanity.

Use cases of AI and blockchain

AI and blockchain, once you use them in tandem, have the potential to bring in significant, unique advancements that can positively impact the future of humanity. The collaboration of AI and blockchain is characterized by decentralization, transparency, heightened security, privacy, and above all, incredible efficiency. By combining the strengths of these technologies, we can work towards a world that is more inclusive, sustainable, and responsive to the evolving needs of humans.

Now, it is time to look at some practical use cases of this combination. We have already discussed the convergence of blockchain and AI and some potential applications to some extent before, but this time, our goal is to understand how exactly the two technologies work together in various industries and their use cases.

Here are the use cases:

  • Enhanced data security and privacy: The decentralized and tamper-proof ledger of blockchain can provide a secure and transparent means of managing personal data for users. Further, AI can be employed for identity verification and access control. This ensures that complete decentralization is maintained, and individual users are the only ones to have control over their data; this practice can help protect data privacy and reduce the risk of data breaches.
  • Financial inclusion: The implementation of blockchain in finance is perhaps the easiest one, given its history with crypto-based financial systems. Blockchain-powered financial products and services can provide banking services to the unbanked and underbanked populations with their heightened accessibility. Blockchain enables secure and efficient cross-border payments, potentially at much lower costs than traditional systems. Further, AI can enhance fraud detection and risk assessment, the duo making financial services more accessible and secure for everyone.
  • Cross-border transactions: In cross-border finance we know that blockchain facilitates faster and cheaper cross-border transactions. Here, AI can chip in to assist in automating compliance checks and enhancing the security of these blockchain-fueled transactions, making international value transfers more efficient.
  • Improved healthcare: Healthcare is one area designated to improve from all of the emerging technologies of today. Blockchains, as you can imagine, can securely store and manage medical records, while AI can analyze these records to offer more accurate diagnoses and treatment recommendations. The combination leads to more effective and personalized healthcare, potentially saving lives and reducing the costs of having several medical professionals and other human interventions involved. Blockchain ensures the privacy and integrity factor in medical record-keeping, while AI brings enhanced patient care by providing insights into medical conditions and treatment options.
  • Supply chain transparency: Yet another area to have something to take from all significant technological advances of today is the supply chain management zone. Blockchain can trace the journey of all products from source to destination, and AI can be used to analyze this collected data to predict and prevent supply chain disruptions, ensure product quality, and reduce waste. The combination of AI and blockchain can lead to safer and more efficient supply chains. So, for instance, AI can predict demand patterns with historical data, and proactively prevent any consecutive disruptions. Blockchain does what it does best: records the product's journey, ensuring transparency and insight into all procedures on the chain. Meanwhile AI is integrated for work like optimized inventory management and predicting when restocking is needed, foregoing manual intervention.
  • Environmental conservation: Blockchain can track the origin of all products with the supply chain integration, ensuring that sustainable and eco-friendly practices are followed. AI can further monitor and optimize energy usage and resource management across organizations and supply chains. The combination of blockchain and AI here can contribute to more responsible environmental practices.
  • Education and qualifications: AI-driven educational platforms can offer personalized learning experiences, increasing access to education worldwide. Further, blockchain can verify the authenticity of educational credentials and certificates, making it easier for individuals to prove their qualifications when it comes to recruitment procedures. Moreover, AI and blockchain can work together to create decentralized, secure research networks. Here, researchers can share their collected data and collaborate more effectively.
  • Transparent voting systems and efficient governance: Blockchain can magnify transparency and security in elections and government processes with optimized voting systems. In governance, AI can improve the efficiency and accuracy of administrative tasks, reduce bureaucratic inefficiencies, and ensure fairness. When combined, blockchain ensures transparency while AI works to streamline administrative processes and election, and both enhance governance in general, creating a win-win situation.
  • AI ethics and accountability: Coming to one of the most pressing issues related to AI, blockchain can provide a transparent and immutable record of the data being fed to an AI system, and AI decision-making processes. This helps with holding the AI systems accountable and addressing ethical concerns relevant to AI applications. When AI systems interact with the blockchain, the decisions taken by AI are time-stamped and cannot be altered as the AI systems learn better decision-making in the future. The AI systems can also utilize blockchain to create transactions among different systems (similar to cryptocurrency transactions) as one system utilizes the products/services of another system. For example, an autonomous car that is facing a flat or ruptured tire in a remote location can order and pay for a new tire and, in parallel, utilize an instant pick up and drop service to get the tire to the location of the car and book an appointment with mechanic who could come to the location to change the tires. As an alternative, it could have just booked a towing service as well.
  • Security of smart contracts: We are fast approaching a future where smart contracts will be in widespread use, and while they automate payments, audits to ensure the security of these smart contracts become more necessary than ever. We do not want instances where due to an error or a breach in a smart contract’s code, payments do not reach their rightful recipients. AI can be used here, to detect any flaws in smart contracts. AI can be trained to understand which issues in a smart contract’s code to flag, and which sequences of events might lead to a draining of funds. So, while blockchain works to facilitate quick, automated transactions, AI can ensure complete safety of smart contracts involved. However, this is not to say the necessity of human auditors is eliminated. While AI can watch out for human error and bad actors, human intelligence is still necessary for comprehensive evaluations.
  • Legal industry: Blockchain can automate and secure legal agreements with the help of smart contracts here, while AI can review legal documents for accuracy and compliance. Blockchain ensures security and immutability for legal contracts, while AI increases the speed and efficiency of legal document review processes.
  • Real estate: Blockchain can simplify property transactions as compared to the traditional way. In fact, real estate properties can be represented as digital tokens on blockchains and can be traded fractionally or wholly. Meanwhile, AI can assess property values, predict market trends, and automate due diligence through integration with blockchain. Blockchain ensures secure and transparent property transactions through digital tokens, while AI enhances real estate decision-making. The two technologies combined can do away with the need for brokers, and other human intervention.
  • News, content verification and copyright protection: Deepfakes and misinformation in news and other content we consume have been a pressing concern in the 21st century. However, using public blockchains to track the origin of any information can be a good way to increase credibility in news. Thus, blockchains can be used to bring the trust factor in content, while AI can also be put to use to detect fake news and copyright violations. Blockchain will ensure content authenticity while AI identifies and mitigates the spread of misinformation and copyright violations.
  • Interactive NFTs: Non-fungible tokens are taking the world by storm, emphasizing authenticity and ownership for blockchain tokens. Partnered with AI, NFTs centered around art can become interactive and dynamic, which would introduce a whole new level of interactivity to NFT enthusiasts. While blockchains help create and distribute digital artwork and characters, AI can enhance their interactive capabilities. If you could note, the factors common in most of these use cases are blockchain providing decentralization, security, transparency, and immutability, while AI enhances the decision-making process and automates tasks, overall leading to procedures more efficient, trusted, and safe across industries.

As the synergy between blockchain and AI evolves, yet more innovative solutions lie on the horizon.

Benefits of AI in decentralized applications

Now, aside from smart contracts, the Ethereum blockchain added one more integral aspect to the blockchains of today—decentralized applications (dApps). For all the use cases of blockchain and AI mentioned above, the integration of AI would be done with a blockchain-based decentralized app or dApp for the two to work together effectively.

Applying AI to improve dApps can bring several notable benefits in general, enhancing their functionality, user experience, and overall performance. The UX part in particular is what most dApps of today struggle with, as they are not yet easily accessible to the technologically inarticulate.

Let us take a quick look at the key advantages of applying AI to dApps:

  • Personalization: AI can analyze user behavior and preferences to deliver personalized content and recommendations within dApps, enhancing user experience.
  • Chatbots and virtual assistants: AI-powered chatbots can offer real-time support and assist users in navigating and using dApps effectively, again adding to a smooth user experience.
  • Automation: AI can automate various dApp functions, such as the processing of transactions, data management, and notifications, reducing the need for manual intervention and enhancing overall efficiency. Further, AI can assist by automating the initiation of trades according to market conditions and other tasks for those new to decentralized finance in particular.
  • Language support: AI-powered NLP can break language barriers by enabling dApps to talk to users in their preferred languages, again adding to the enhanced UX aspect.
  • Predictive analysis: From analyzing the data generated by dApps, AI can predict trends, potential issues, and user behavior within the dApp, allowing for proactive problem-solving and required optimizations.
  • Data analytics: Further, AI can provide valuable insights which can inform and enrich decision-making, business strategies, and user engagement efforts.
  • Threat and fraud detection: AI algorithms can detect any suspicious or fraudulent activities and alert users or administrators, preventing unauthorized access or malicious actions. This would enhance the security factor for dApps.
  • Scalability: AI can help in scaling dApps based on usage patterns, ensuring that the network can handle increased traffic at certain periods of time without compromising performance.
  • Authenticity: AI can verify the authenticity of content and data on dApps, reducing the risk of counterfeiyted or manipulated information.
  • Moderation and filtering: AI can also filter out harmful or inappropriate content on decentralized applications, ensuring a safer, inclusive, and more user-friendly environment.
  • dApp health monitoring: With its analysis of generated data, AI can also predict potential issues in a dApp’s performance, allowing for preventive maintenance and minimizing downtime.

As the role in dApp optimizes, the general benefits of AI in blockchain are increasing. Therefore, while the converged technologies can enhance various procedures across a range of industries, they can inherently make each other better as well, through magnifying the core aspects of one another.

Examples of AI-blockchain convergence

Now let us take a look at some unique usage of AI and blockchain combined organizations around the world have thought up:

  • The Web3 domain company Unstoppable Domains speaks of an idea of theirs where blockchain can be used to track the source of news and quotes to the person involved. So, person X can employ an AI bot to scour the internet for mentions of their name, and any time they are quoted or mentioned by a news outlet, they can receive a notification to verify the information on the blockchain using their decentralized domain name attached to their crypto wallet and private key.
  • According to the data tokenization company Nevermined, the AI and blockchain combination can do away with plagiarism of content and copyright issues. Smart contracts on the blockchain can automatically impose copyright terms, and AI algorithms can be put in place to scan content for any possible infringement. There would be an immutable record of ownership and usage of any given content of the blockchain, which would allow creators to share their work in a secure fashion. Nevermined is already in the process of testing out this concept.
  • Alethea AI has created intelligent NFTs or iNFTs that can combine an NFT you create with Intelligence Pods. These pods can be trained to develop a personality you want and essentially give your NFT a rounded-up character. iNFTs are customizable to the unique dataset of a NFT, channeling everything from a comprehensive personality to a knowledge set to certain skills.
  • The generative AI company Chain ML is already working with a web3 security company called Cube3 to put an end to unethical crypto transactions with AI. As Chain ML acknowledges, the potential of AI in securing smart contracts is incredible.

All four of these examples bring to reality some of the use cases of the convergence of blockchain and AI. As we have mentioned, the horizon is infinite for the combination of the two emerging technologies, and new use cases will continue to emerge and be brought to life.

Conclusion

In this chapter, we learned all about the combination of AI and blockchain, how the two converge for unique use cases, and the benefits of the two. We explored the dynamic synergy between AI and blockchain technology, highlighted their strengths and weaknesses and how these two powerful technologies correlate to some real-world examples. From scalability to personalization, AI and blockchain technologies are here to evolve and bring more examples to life.

In the next chapter, we will cover the convergence of AI and robotics. We will look at how artificial intelligence controls robots to perform complex tasks and make decisions on the spot and understand the core aspects of the interweaving of both technologies.

Questions

    Answer: AI can automate repetitive tasks and take over in hazardous areas of work for increased efficiency and safety of their human counterparts. Further, this way, humans can concern themselves with the more creative fields.

  2. What are some strengths of blockchain that will be beneficial to humans going forward?

CHAPTER 8The Convergence of AI and Robotics

Introduction

AI is truly the one technology redefining the boundaries of human achievement in this day and age. We have already discussed the effects of AI combined with blockchain, and now it is time to move on to understanding the convergence of AI and robotics. How does adding intelligence on par with (or close to) humans to the robotic machine change things up, and how does it impact the future of technology and our world? How does AI empower robotic machines and what are the key technologies involved in drones, facial recognition, chatbots, virtual assistants, etc.? We find all that in this chapter!

Structure

The chapter covers the following topics:

  • AI and robotics in tech conversations
  • Defining robotics and AI
  • History of the combination AI and robotics
  • Applications of robotics and AI
  • Advantages of converging robotics and AI
  • Future of AI and robotics
  • Introducing smart robots

Objectives

The objective of this chapter about the convergence of artificial intelligence and robotics is to analyze how intelligent technology can bring more control and efficiency to the robotic machine and enable it to perform increasingly complex tasks and make decisions based on real-time data and analysis. We will go from the history of AI and automation to understanding the nuances of AI in robots and how intelligent robots work. Further, we will explore the benefits and future of the convergence of AI and robotics. For instance, AI-powered robotics can enhance efficiency and safety for human workers, while AI and robotics combined can provide advanced care in the medical field by tackling patients without human intervention and bringing in personalized services.

Without further ado, let us start by understanding the combination of both technologies, as well as their scopes on their own.

AI and robotics in tech conversations

While robotics and AI have long been topics of discussion, the past two decades have witnessed a remarkable surge in their advancement and utilization. Before the 21st century, there were misconceptions about machines taking over the world and rendering humans useless. However, the progress in both technologies in the past few years has put such irrationalities to rest. Robots and AI now work in harmony with humans, enhancing our productivity, speed, and accuracy.

The current buzz surrounding the fusion of AI and Robotics is primarily due to their pivotal role in propelling the Fourth Industrial Revolution, often referred to as Industry 4.0. This intersection of AI and robotics is set to be a key driver in realizing the Industry 4.0 vision, which centers on enhancing manufacturing efficiency and flexibility by deploying cyber-physical systems, cognitive capabilities, and IoT technology, all underpinned by artificial intelligence.

The increased efficiency achieved through the integration of AI and robotics within smart factories is fueling a growing adoption of these technologies within the manufacturing sector. This significant shift has prompted the World Economic Forum (WEF) to identify the emergence of Robotics and AI as the fourth wave of the industrial revolution.

Now, outside of manufacturing, the convergence of AI and robotics holds the potential to revolutionize many other industries. Before we get into their combined possibilities, let us go back to the basics for a bit: what are robotics and AI?

Defining robotics and AI

Robotics, as we know, is a multidisciplinary field of technology and engineering that focuses on the design, construction, operation, and use of machines known as robots. Autonomous or semi-autonomous machines can perform tasks in the physical world, often in situations that may be hazardous, monotonous, or impractical for humans. Robotics combines elements of mechanical engineering, electrical engineering, computer science, and AI to create machines capable of sensing their environment, making decisions, and taking actions to accomplish specific objectives. However, without AI integration, robots are incapable of decision-making in changing environments.

Robots come in various forms, from industrial robots used in manufacturing to social robots designed for human interaction. They can be equipped with sensors, cameras, and other perception devices to gather information from the environment, and they use algorithms and software to process this information, make decisions, and control their movements.

The field of robotics encompasses a wide range of applications, including automation in manufacturing, reinforcement learning in AI, surgical robots in healthcare, automated harvesting in agriculture, AI-powered data analysis in space exploration, and more.

Differences between robotics and AI

Before we go into studying the convergence of robotics and artificial intelligence, it is important to understand the differences between the two. Further, all of us may have noticed instances where the terms AI and robotics have been used interchangeably. Robotics and AI are quite distinct but interconnected fields, and the basic differences between the two are important to note to clearly comprehend the scope of the two technologies.

Let us see the basic differences between robotics and AI:

Robotics

AI

Definition

Robotics is the broader field that deals with the design, construction, and operation of physical machines known as robots. These machines can be programmed to perform tasks autonomously or semi-autonomously, with the help of AI integration.

AI focuses on creating software systems or algorithms with intelligence resembling humans. These systems can perform tasks typically requiring human intelligence, such as problem-solving, pattern recognition, and decision-making. AI does not necessarily always involve physical machines.

Scope

The field of robotics is primarily concerned with the development of physical and mechanical devices. Robots interact with the physical world for special tasks and can include aspects like sensors, actuators, and mobility.

AI focuses on the development of intelligent software and algorithms that can analyze data, learn from it, and make independent decisions. AI can be applied across a number of domains, from natural language processing to image recognition.

Area and environment of work

Machines powered by robotics interact directly with the physical environment, and are accordingly capable of relevant navigation and perception.

AI systems, on the other hand, typically interact with data or information digitally and may not have a physical presence as mentioned above. Their basic job is to process data and make decisions based on it. AI systems can be taught to become dynamic and constantly take in and process new data for real time decision making.

Cognitive abilities

Robots can be made to have physical sensors to gather information about the environment, but their cognitive capabilities often rely on any added AI components to process and interpret this data gathered.

AI systems are focused on cognitive tasks like understanding languages, recognizing patterns in data, and making decisions based on this data analysis.

Applications

Robotics is used in various industries such as manufacturing (industrial robots), agriculture (agricultural drones), healthcare (surgical robots), and space exploration (rover robots).

AI is applied in a range of fields too, including natural language processing (chatbots), image and speech recognition (face recognition and virtual assistants), financial services (fraud detection), and healthcare (disease diagnosis).

Examples

We all know Sophia, a social humanoid. She was created in 2016, and really pushed the envelope in terms of what robotics can achieve.

Virtual personal assistants like Siri and Alexa are everyday examples of AI in our lives.

Table 8.1: Differences between robotics and AI

Now, the differences between the two are definitely clear to us, but why the confusion in the first place? This is because there is a significant overlap between robotics and AI. Many robots incorporate AI to enable decision-making and autonomous behavior. AI technologies like ML and computer vision are often used to enhance the capabilities of robots, and many assume AI to be a core part of all robots. While this is true for most new-age robots, the two terms definitely cannot be used in place of one another.

To sum up, the basic difference is that while robotics involves the design and construction of physical machines, AI focuses on creating intelligent software systems. These two fields often intersect, with AI being a critical component of many robots, enabling them to sense, process information, and, most importantly, make decisions. We have seen an increased convergence of robotics and AI over the past few years. The integration of both technologies allows robots to perform tasks and also learn and adapt in real-time to the physical environment. Embodied AI is a concept that combines the computational intelligence of AI with the physical capabilities of a robot to perform tasks. With AI as the brain, it enables a robot to sense, process information, and, most importantly, make decisions. The convergence of such advanced technologies bridges the gap between the real and virtual worlds.

Understanding the convergence of robotics and AI

Now, let us expand on how robotics and AI work together. As we found out, AI brings in techniques that empower robots to process the data they gather themselves, understand the pattern and context, plan the next actions, and execute them. Therefore, robots can move around without sticking to their rigid, one-time programs.

Meanwhile, how does robotics enhance AI? Of course, to a system without a physical body, robotics provides mobility and navigation skills, not to mention object manipulation.

All of this means that AI combined with robotics can create a machine with capabilities actually on par with humans. Further, AI-powered robots can even interact with humans and learn new activities without being programmed since they have the brains to learn from a changing environment and adapt.

Before we learn further about both technologies, do you remember their origin? When did we first see an AI algorithm or a robot resembling what we have today? Let us refresh our memories quickly!

History of the combination AI and robotics

As we know, AI and robotics are the two emerging technologies to receive consistently increasing mainstream attention today. However, their individual and convergent histories tell a story of their existence for quite a while now.

The first relevant concept to emerge was automation. One of the first known automated systems in history was the water clock from ancient Greece. The Greeks began to use water clocks to measure the length of time plays, speeches, or work shifts took around 325 BC. The device was called a clepsydra, or water thief. It was built with clay, stone, or copper.

During the time of the industrial revolution, there were machines developed to automate procedures in factories, reducing the need for a human workforce and increasing efficiency in output. This was still nowhere near to the robots we know today, however.

Anything resembling the robots of the 21st century was first created around the middle of the last century. In 1954, the very first industrial robot called the Unimate, was created by George Devol. This robot was mostly used in manufacturing and magnified productivity in the automotive industry. Later, as we know, the term robot came from science fiction author Isaac Asimov.

Asimov laid out the Three Laws of Robotics, which are a set of guiding principles that robots need to follow to cause any harm to humans. Here is what the laws state:

  • Robots should always put humans first and ensure their safety.
  • Robots should always obey commands from humans, given they do not contradict the first law.
  • Robots should focus on protecting themselves unless it directly contradicts the first or second law.

To date, these principles are adhered to while creating robots to prioritize the human factor.

Meanwhile, the very first AI program was created back in 1951 by Christopher Strachey. Strachey made a checker-playing program for a Ferranti Mark I computer. Soon after 1956, researchers from relevant fields gathered at the Dartmouth Conference to discuss how AI could be developed and its potential. This conference is referred to by many as the birth of AI as its own academic field.

However, AI research gathered momentum only in the 1960s and 1970s, when the development of algorithms like machine learning started. The following decades saw the creation of expert systems and, most importantly, Lisp, a programming language often used in AI research.

After a brief pause in the 1980s, research for AI and automation software picked up steam in the 1990s. This was when inventions like customer service chatbots and speech recognition software came about. Further, the development of the internet brought forth search engines and recommendation systems, all of which required AI algorithms to function to varying extents.

In the 2000s, we had further advancements like big data and advanced ML algorithms, all of which have led to smart virtual assistants, recommendations, and personalization across social media, websites, streaming services, and autonomous vehicles. As of today, both automation and AI have become necessary parts of our daily lives without us realizing it. They are all over the search engines we use to look for information; the social media feeds that curate the content of our choice for us and the robotic help we get in factories and warehouses across industries.

Now, getting back to analyzing the combination of AI and robotics. How exactly does AI empower a robot?

AI enriching a robotic infrastructure

Time to expand a little on how exactly AI systems are integrated into robots. AI technologies make robots smarter by providing them with the following abilities:

  • AI equips robots with sensors and cameras that allow them to perceive their environment. Technologies like machine learning and computer vision algorithms help robots recognize objects, people, and their surroundings. This means robots can see and understand the world (at least to the extent their area of work requires) in a human-like manner.
  • Robots powered by AI can learn from their experiences. They can analyze the data gathered through experience and adjust their behavior accordingly. Just like humans, this constant learning process enables robots to improve their performance over time, making them efficient and capable, even more so than humans.
  • AI technologies allow robots to process large amounts of data and make decisions based on that information. Thus, robots can choose the best actions to take in different situations, which is quite useful in tasks that require problem-solving or decision-making. Gone are the days of robots requiring to be reprogrammed at the slightest change in the work environment.
  • AI algorithms help robots optimize their actions. For example, they can find the most efficient path through a warehouse to pick and pack items or adjust their movements to minimize energy consumption continuously.
  • AI empowers robots to understand and respond to human language. This means they can communicate with people, understand voice commands, and engage in comprehensive conversations, making them very user-friendly and capable of working alongside humans.
  • AI enables robots to take on complex and intricate tasks that used to be reserved only for humans. This includes tasks in healthcare, manufacturing, research, and exploration.
  • AI-driven robots have infiltrated various fields across the globe, such as manufacturing, transport, agriculture, healthcare, and more. With AI, robots can identify faces, understand natural language commands, analyze human speech, do predictive analysis, and adapt. The healthcare sector has witnessed a tremendous transformation with the integration of AI technology. Today, AI-powered surgical robots are predicting diagnosis, patient outcomes, and surgical efficiency. The integrated algorithms analyze real-time patient data to provide surgeons with accurate physiological details and also automate certain surgical steps. These robots can now perform several complex procedures with utmost accuracy and precision, making them safer.

As you can gather, AI technologies overall provide robots with cognitive capabilities that allow them to sense, think, and act in a manner that is closer to human intelligence. Thus, robots become smarter, more adaptable, and extremely valuable in various industries and applications.

AI technologies used in robotics

Now, which specific AI technologies are put into robots to create autonomous systems capable of all the feats mentioned above? Here are some of them:

  • Artificial neural networks: Neural networks, including deep neural networks, are used in robots for tasks like decision-making, pattern recognition, and precise control.
  • Machine learning: ML algorithms enable robots to learn from data and improve their performance over time. This is vital for tasks like object recognition, speech recognition, and path planning. Reinforcement learning, an area of ML, is often used to teach robots to make decisions and take actions based on rewards and punishments.
  • Computer vision: Computer vision algorithms help robots see, per se, and interpret their environment. This technology is essential for robots to perform tasks like object detection, tracking, and facial recognition. Deep neural networks are usually used for image processing in robotics.
  • Sensor fusion: Robots often have multiple sensors, such as cameras, lidar (light detection and ranging), ultrasonic sensors, and gyroscopes. Sensor fusion techniques, which involve combining data from various sensors, help robots create a more comprehensive understanding of their environment, which is important for them to take in data.
  • Natural language processing (NLP): Robots equipped with this technology can understand and generate human language, as you can assume. This technology is useful for human-robot interaction, enabling robots to process voice commands and engage in conversations with users in a human-like way.
  • Simultaneous, Localization and Mapping (SLAM): SLAM algorithms can enable robots to create maps of their surroundings while determining their own position within that map. SLAM is crucial in robotics for autonomous navigation and exploration tasks.
  • Transformer models: Transformer models are revolutionizing robotics by enabling machines to learn from enormous amounts of data. These models can process the sequence of images and natural languages to induce certain actions.
  • Reinforcement learning: Reinforcement learning (RL) is a branch of ML where an agent makes a decision to maximize a cumulative reward through an interactive process (exploration and exploitation). RL enables robots to interact directly with evolving environments, learn complex behaviors, and adapt to them.
  • Path planning: Path planning algorithms help robots, as you can guess, find the optimal route from one point to another while avoiding obstacles. These algorithms are especially essential for mobile robots, including autonomous vehicles and drones.
  • Reactive control: Reactive control systems allow robots to react quickly to changing situations and avoid collisions. They are often used in conjunction with path planning algorithms.
  • Predictive analytics: Predictive analytics, used most often in industrial robots, helps the machines predict equipment failures and maintenance needs by analyzing data taken in by various sensors.
  • Behavior trees: Behavior trees are used to design the more complex robot behaviors by breaking them down into smaller, manageable tasks. They are very useful in applications like humanoid robots.

All these different AI technologies empower robots to interact with and adapt to their environments, perform a wide range of tasks as demanded by their industries, and make autonomous decisions. As AI continues to advance, robotics is also making great strides as robots become more capable and fit for everyday tasks.

Applications of robotics and AI

So now we know how AI fits into a robotic machine to lend it human-like intelligence and, overall, create systems that can work alongside humans across industries. Now, which industries stand to benefit the most from the combined applications of robotics and AI, let us discuss those:

  • Manufacturing: The applications of the technology convergence in question have numerous uses in manufacturing. AI-powered robots can be deployed in manufacturing for:

    • High-precision assembly line roles
    • Working with human operators
    • Picking up items and reshelving them quickly and repetitively
    • Tracking inventory levels and sounding alerts for replenishment of material
    • Predictive maintenance with data gathered from sensors and reducing downtime
    • Any repetitive work that requires patience and precision

    AI-fueled robots can essentially reduce the human error factor in manufacturing tasks and automate repetitive tasks, freeing human workers and reducing labor expenses.

  • Healthcare: Yet another sector where the high precision of robots guided by AI can come in handy. Here is how AI and robotics enhance healthcare:

    • Robots powered by AI can assist surgeons for better accuracy and efficiency.
    • AI-powered robots can be lent mobility for gauging which medications would be needed in advance and transporting them by themselves.
    • Hospital staff can be freed up with AI-fueled robots carrying lab samples and supplies.
    • Finally, AI and robotics powered exoskeletons can give mobility back to patients after they receive temporarily or permanently paralyzing injuries.
    • For the elderly, these robots can be the perfect partners both in terms of medical and social assistance.

    AI and robotics in healthcare can overall amplify the efficiency and precision factors, and provide comprehensive care to patients.

  • Enhanced security: Let us see how AI-powered robots can help here:

    • AI-powered patrol robots can monitor designated areas and watch out for any unusual occurrence.
    • AI can empower robots to conduct behavioral analysis to identify threats before any mishaps.
    • Facial recognition is another handy technology AI and robotics can bring while patrolling live or scanning surveillance footage.

    AI-powered robots can be deployed in defense to work alongside armed officers and ensure security and surveillance with the precision humans may lack.

  • Transportation and logistics: Here is how AI-powered robots are integrated in this particular area:

    • Self-driving vehicles can use AI for situational awareness, detecting incoming hazards, and planning routes for optimal navigation.
    • Transit assistants can be brought forth to help humans with navigation guidance and ensure their safety in busy roads.
    • Further, AI-fueled robots can handle last-mile deliveries of food and other goods.
    • AI scheduling systems can optimize routes, fuel usage, and traffic coordination throughout transportation networks.

    AI-enabled robots can revolutionize transportation and at the same time, be a navigation guide for humans for their safekeeping.

  • Agriculture: In farming, AI and robotics can work wonders again like so:
    • Autonomous drones can be enabled with computer vision so they can survey crops for health checks, track growth, and figure out irrigation necessities.
    • Robots can be used to sort out and pick ripe produce.
    • Autonomous tractors can put in seeds, apply water and pesticides, and do more, all by their own decision-making.
    • AI-powered robots can do small jobs like weed reduction to optimize processes and ensure healthy crop growth.

AI is already used in agriculture and across supply chains around the world. Adding robotics to the mix would elevate the convenience and efficiency to another level entirely.

Now, aside from these notable areas, here are some more industries that AI and robotics can revolutionize together.

  • AI and robotics in space exploration: Robots like the Mars Exploration Rovers Spirit and Opportunity use AI to explore distant planets. They autonomously navigate challenging terrain, collect samples, and transmit data back to Earth.
  • AI and robotics to enhance entertainment: Theme parks and entertainment venues can feature AI-driven animatronics and robots that provide immersive experiences, enhancing visitor engagement.
  • AI and robotics for environmental conservation: AI-powered robots can be used to monitor and protect the environment. They can track wildlife, identify and address pollution, and contribute to conservation efforts.
  • AI and robotics in agriculture: AI analysis tools can precisely enable the spraying of insecticides and pesticides based on real-time crop investigation data. Similarly, AI-based robots can harvest crops efficiently with accuracy, leading to better yields and sustainable practices.
  • AI and robotics in disaster response: These technologies play a key role in disaster response (floods, accidents, landslides, etc.), by analyzing data sets in real-time, ensuring timely interventions, coordinating emergency responses, and minimizing the disaster impact.

All these applications demonstrate the versatility of combining AI and robotics to solve real-world challenges and enhance multiple aspects of our lives. The synergy between these technologies will continue to drive innovation in multiple industries, and gradually achieve more widespread usage.

Advantages of converging robotics and AI

Now, we already have a good idea of the many benefits of integrating robotics with AI, from all the applications mentioned above. Still, here is a quick look at the range of benefits the combination of the two offers:

  • AI empowers robots to perceive their work environments and engage in decision-making instead of requiring reprogramming at the smallest change.
  • AI-powered robots have a much higher degree of autonomy.
  • Since they can learn in real time, they can improve their ways of working by themselves instead of requiring further programming or training.
  • AI-powered robots can communicate with humans more effectively, increasing efficiency where both have to work side by side.
  • AI-powered robots can tackle hazardous situations with their own thinking abilities without putting humans in danger.
  • Instead of getting wary of repetitive work, the constant tuning of the same motions helps AI-powered robots produce the maximum possible efficiency in output. This is especially important for industries with stringent quality standards.
  • Automation with AI-driven robots can reduce labor costs and minimize errors, ultimately leading to long-term cost savings.
  • Further, companies can achieve higher production rates with fewer resources.
  • AI-fueled robots can work continuously without breaks, enabling around-the-clock operations; this is particularly valuable in applications at data centers and for warehousing and surveillance.
  • Robots can collect vast amounts of data through sensors and cameras, and AI enables them to analyze this collected data for insights, facilitating predictive maintenance, process optimization, and data-driven decision-making.
  • AI enables robots to autonomously navigate environments, plan routes, and avoid obstacles, which is crucial for applications like self-driving cars and drones.
  • Last but not least, the convergence of AI and robotics allows for heightened scalability, where additional robots can be very easily integrated into existing systems to meet increased demand.

Potential drawbacks of converging robotics and AI are as follows:

  • The integration of AI and robotics raises significant ethical issues, such as privacy concerns, algorithmic bias, accountability for robot decisions, and adherence to ethical frameworks.
  • The complexity of AI-powered robots can make understanding and managing these technologies more difficult. With more sophisticated technology, reliability may be compromised, resulting in failures of critical systems.
  • Robots in public environments may raise safety concerns for humans if it does not respond appropriately or immediately to critical situations in times of natural disasters or accidents.

With these benefits and more, the convergence of AI and robotics continues to drive innovation, leading to optimized applications in essentially any field you can think of!

Future of AI and robotics

The convergence of robotics and AI is all set to reshape industries around the globe, along with our daily lives. AI-powered robots have much to contribute in areas like education, where humanoid robots can bring about personalized learning experiences. Meanwhile, there can be assistant robots at home. While they are at it, AI-fueled robots can set out to solve the biggest problems humanity faces right now, from climate change to healthcare issues to energy conservation. The Chinese Ministry of Industry and Information Technology (MIIT) believes that humanoids will likely become the next disruptive technology, like computers or smartphones. Ethical AI and governance, advanced AI processors, and improved algorithm efficiency are some key trends to look out for in the near future.

Overall, AI-powered robots bring hopes of an equitable and fairer society, empowering humans from all walks of life. However, there are challenges to be addressed and solved before we can move on to that future. There is the fact that these technologies are still far from affordable to the common man, and there are ethical considerations as well. Firstly, we would need a widespread revolution like that of the smartphone from the 2010s. Further, developing the relevant policies and governance to guide the rise of AI and robotics would be necessary.

Once the right balance between innovation, ethical considerations, and regulation has been found, we are looking at some massively changing social dynamics as intelligent robots enter our daily lives!

Now, speaking of advancements in AI-powered robotics. While the future we just spoke of is still some distance away, smart robots are already here, and they are taking over the world, one industry at a time. Want to learn more about them? Let us dive in!

Introducing smart robots

As you may be able to gauge from the name alone, smart robots are indeed simply robots equipped with AI. Smart robots are existing applications of the convergence of AI and robotics that we can study as an example of what the merging of these two technologies can truly achieve.

Expanding on the definition a little, smart robots are powered by multiple highly sensitive sensors that take note of the environment around them and are equipped with features like self-learning and self-correction, all thanks to the AI technology integrated. A smart robot is able to work with humans and emulate their actions.

An early example of a smart robot would be Baxter, created by Rethink Robotics of Boston, Massachusetts. The company is no longer in operation. Baxter, the humanoid robot, was an industrial android who worked next to employees in factories, and he was specifically crafted for super repetitive tasks like precision packing. His friendly demeanor made him quite popular.

Since Baxter, the number and kinds of tasks that can be done by smart robots have steadily increased, and now they are equipped with capabilities not only of manual labor but also of cognitive work.

Benefits of smart robots

There are quite a few advantages of adopting smart robots. For instance:

  • They work fast and with precision, completing highly repetitive tasks with efficiency and no human error.
  • Along with the precision, they are equipped with intelligence akin to a human in their area of work without requiring repeated programming.
  • They can learn dynamically from their environment and experience in their area of operation.
  • Automation with smart robots can reduce labor costs, minimize errors, and lower operational expenses in the long run.
  • Smart robots are designed to work in hazardous environments, reducing risks to human workers. They can effortlessly take on tasks such as bomb disposal, deep-sea exploration, and nuclear reactor inspection.
  • Smart robots are equipped with sensors, and AI can collect and analyze vast amounts of data, providing valuable insights for decision-making and process optimization.
  • Smart robots can be easily scaled up or down to keep pace with the demands of a specific task or production process.

Growing demand for smart robots

There are many reasons why the development of smart robots is largely in demand in today’s time. A couple of them would be as follows:

  • Industries like manufacturing, healthcare, construction, and even automobiles are increasingly reliant on industrial automation. Smart robots are especially revered in these sectors due to their maximized accuracy and consistent performance. In all of these industries, smart robots can contribute with superior quality in work, and they can also keep workers safe from any injuries due to hazardous circumstances or machines going haywire. Further, smart robots can be programmed to do specific jobs like moving heavy materials, heavily adding to the productivity factor.
  • The age of the Internet of Things (IoT) is nigh, and various machines and systems can now digitally communicate by themselves to elevate the quality of life for humans. Predictive maintenance can now forecast and prevent issues before they can happen. With IoT, large amounts of data can be stored, and smart robots can take care of predictive maintenance. They can identify when any machines might break down by analyzing data from sensors that monitor things like temperature and voltage in the machines.

From these reasons, you could say that the primary reasons for smart robots being in so much demand are:

  • Increased accuracy and precision in work
  • Protecting human workers
  • Check in on and fix machines required for daily work before they have a chance to malfunction and cause disruptions

Overall, smart robots are required to improve the quality of life for mankind, so regular tasks are automated as much as possible.

Areas of application for the smart robots

There are many industries where smart robots can be put to work. Here are the top sectors where smart robots can bring massive improvements:

  • In healthcare: Smart robots can be stand-ins for primary care physicians 24/7. They can be programmed to interact with the patients in a humane fashion, identify symptoms and provide basic evaluations and accordingly start treatments, and further gauge the need for advanced care. Smart robots in healthcare can also put patients to ease by providing them a space to discuss uncomfortable health conditions and prescribe medicines to them, all the while preserving their privacy.
  • Industrial usage: Smart robots in manufacturing units can work wonders. They can expand the capacity of factories, keep human workers safe by tackling the more dangerous tasks, cut down on labor costs, and of course, amplify both quality and quantity of the products. In the upcoming years, smart robots are expected to be greatly deployed across the global manufacturing industry.
  • Education: Educational robots serve as practical teaching aids to students. They are used to engage with students and develop problem-solving, reasoning, and communication skills.
  • Disaster monitoring: Advanced aerial drones powered by AI can survey disaster-prone areas, examine the damage, identify survivors, and promote quicker response to deploy necessary resources at the precise location.
  • Warehouse management: Robots have been extensively used in warehouses to optimize inventory, order fulfilment, monitor supply chains, improve productivity, and reduce physical strain on workers.
  • Others: Virtually all sectors from agriculture to logistics to defense and security to law enforcement to public relations can benefit from the deployment of smart robots. This ensures heightened safety while maximized efficiency is ensured.

Future of smart robots

As you can tell, the lines between the different emerging technologies have started to blur, and the interlinking of technologies like AI, robotics, and automation is inevitable. In the context of smart robots, they are just one of the many examples of what such technological convergences can accomplish. Such technologies will continue to be widely adopted around the world, and they will increase productivity across industries and operations. As smart robots continue to fuel digital transformation, the futuristic society we see in movies may not be very far off, where such systems bring in the highest degree of convenience in human existence across all classes of society.

However, there are rising concerns on societal impact and certain ethical consideration in the adoption of smart robots. The integration of smart robots in various sectors may lead to major job displacement in the manual labor positions. This significant change may impact the economy. Another challenge is the ethical implications regarding the accountability of decision making of smart robots. Smart robots must align with the values of the society and norms without harming the human rights. It is, therefore, necessary to establish clear ethical guidelines and regulatory frameworks to address these challenges.

Conclusion

And now, at long last, we have finished our initial learnings about AI-enabled robotics. As we went through the definitions of robotics and AI and the intricacies of their convergence, it must have gotten increasingly clear to you that the scope of these technologies combined is infinite. As AI makes robots capable of intelligence and efficiency not only on par with, but beyond that of humans, a world of convenience and equity lies in wait for humans. All that is left is to solve the pressing issues, and initiate the technological revolution spearheaded by AI.

In the next chapter, we discuss one of these very issues: the privacy and security concerns of AI, blockchain, and robotics. We will discuss the exchange of your data in a trustless environment brought by these technologies, and how privacy and security can be ensured with the help of the very same technological advancements.

In this chapter, we have found out the definitions and differences of robotics and AI, gone through the history of this combination, found out about the technologies that bring them together, and discussed the use cases in healthcare, agriculture, space exploration, education, disaster monitoring, and more. Finally, we have also speculated about the future this combination may have, which includes augmentation of human capabilities, intelligent algorithms, humanoids in workforce, etc.

While the humanoid Sophia is a social robot (empowered with AI), Amazon’s Alexa is an AI system that does not require a robotic body to complete its actions.
